## F. Python Programming

*Chapter 4 includes Python examples in the textbook. Here are the complete programs:*

### Program 1: List Operations

```python
items = ["Decorations", "Snacks", "Cold drinks", "Plates", "Balloons"]
print("Original list:", items)

# Insertion
items.insert(0, "Invite friends")
print("After insert:", items)

# Deletion
items.remove("Snacks")
print("After remove:", items)

# Searching
if "Cold drinks" in items:
    print("Cold drinks is on the list.")
else:
    print("Cold drinks is not on the list.")
```

**Output:**
```
Original list: ['Decorations', 'Snacks', 'Cold drinks', 'Plates', 'Balloons']
After insert: ['Invite friends', 'Decorations', 'Snacks', 'Cold drinks', 'Plates', 'Balloons']
After remove: ['Invite friends', 'Decorations', 'Cold drinks', 'Plates', 'Balloons']
Cold drinks is on the list.
```

**Explanation:** Demonstrates list insertion, deletion, and searching.

**Roman Urdu:** List mein insert, remove, aur search dikhaya.

### Program 2: Stack Implementation

```python
stack_of_books = []
print("Initial stack:", stack_of_books)

stack_of_books.append("Book A")
print("After pushing 'Book A':", stack_of_books)

stack_of_books.append("Book B")
print("After pushing 'Book B':", stack_of_books)

top_book = stack_of_books.pop()
print("Removed book:", top_book)
print("Stack after popping:", stack_of_books)
```

**Output:**
```
Initial stack: []
After pushing 'Book A': ['Book A']
After pushing 'Book B': ['Book A', 'Book B']
Removed book: Book B
Stack after popping: ['Book A']
```

**Explanation:** Stack uses `append()` for push and `pop()` for pop. LIFO is demonstrated.

**Roman Urdu:** Stack mein `append()` se push aur `pop()` se pop. LIFO dikhaya.

### Program 3: Queue Implementation

```python
from queue import Queue

q = Queue()
q.put("Ahmed")
q.put("Fatima")

front_person = q.queue[0]
print("Front person:", front_person)

removed_person = q.get()
print("Removed person:", removed_person)

q.put("Sara")
print("Updated queue:", list(q.queue))
```

**Output:**
```
Front person: Ahmed
Removed person: Ahmed
Updated queue: ['Fatima', 'Sara']
```

**Explanation:** Queue uses `put()` for enqueue and `get()` for dequeue. FIFO is demonstrated.

**Roman Urdu:** Queue mein `put()` se enqueue aur `get()` se dequeue. FIFO dikhaya.

## G. Important Formulas and Rules

*No mathematical formulas in this chapter. Key rules:*

1. **Stack:** LIFO — Last-In, First-Out
2. **Queue:** FIFO — First-In, First-Out
3. **List Index:** Starts at 0
4. **Tree Height:** Longest path from root to leaf
5. **Graph Degree:** Number of edges connected to a vertex

**Roman Urdu:** Stack LIFO, Queue FIFO, List index 0 se, Tree height root se leaf tak, Graph degree edges ki tadaad.

## H. Textbook Exercise Solutions

### Multiple Choice Questions (MCQs)

**1. The function used to add an item at the end of a list in Python:**
a) insert()
b) append()
c) remove()
d) pop()

**Answer: b) append()**
**Explanation:** `append()` adds an item to the end of a list.
**Roman Urdu:** `append()` list ke end mein item add karta hai.

**2. The purpose of the `in` keyword used with a Python list:**
a) Adds an item to the list
b) Removes an item from the list
c) Checks if an item exists in the list
d) Returns the length of the list

**Answer: c) Checks if an item exists in the list**
**Explanation:** `in` is used to check membership.
**Roman Urdu:** `in` check karta hai ke item list mein hai ya nahi.

**3. An operation that removes an item from the top of the stack:**
a) Push
b) Pop
c) Peek
d) Add

**Answer: b) Pop**
**Explanation:** `pop()` removes the top item from a stack.
**Roman Urdu:** `pop()` stack ke top se item remove karta hai.

**4. The operation used to add an item to a queue:**
a) Dequeue
b) Peek
c) Enqueue
d) Remove

**Answer: c) Enqueue**
**Explanation:** Enqueue adds an item to the back of a queue.
**Roman Urdu:** Enqueue queue ke peeche item add karta hai.

**5. True statement about the height of a tree:**
a) Number of edges from the root to the deepest node
b) Number of nodes from the root to the deepest node
c) Number of children of the root node
d) Always equal to the number of nodes in the tree

**Answer: a) Number of edges from the root to the deepest node**
**Explanation:** Height is the longest path (edges) from root to farthest leaf.
**Roman Urdu:** Height root se sab se door leaf tak edges ki tadaad hai.

**6. A scenario where a graph data structure is most suitable:**
a) Managing a to-do list
b) Modeling a line of customers in a store
c) Representing connections in a social network
d) All of the above

**Answer: c) Representing connections in a social network**
**Explanation:** Graphs model networks of connections — social networks are a classic example.
**Roman Urdu:** Graph social network jaise connections ke liye best hai.

---

### Short Questions

**1. Explain how the 'insert()' function works in Python lists. Provide an example.**

**Answer:** `insert(index, item)` adds an item at a specific index, shifting other items to the right. Example: `my_list = [1, 2, 3]; my_list.insert(1, 5)` results in `[1, 5, 2, 3]`.

**Roman Urdu:** `insert(index, item)` specific index par item add karta hai, baqi items shift ho jate hain.

**2. Explain the potential issues which could arise when two variables reference the same list in a program? Provide an example.**

**Answer:** When two variables reference the same list, changes made through one variable affect the other because they point to the same memory location. Example: `a = [1, 2, 3]; b = a; b.append(4)` — now `a` also becomes `[1, 2, 3, 4]`. To avoid this, use `b = a.copy()`.

**Roman Urdu:** Do variables ek hi list ko reference karein to ek mein change doosre mein bhi dikhta hai. `copy()` se bach sakte hain.

**3. Define a stack and explain the Last-In, First-Out (LIFO) principle.**

**Answer:** A stack is a data structure where items are added and removed from the top only. LIFO means the last item added is the first one removed. Example: stack of books — the top book (last placed) is the first taken off.

**Roman Urdu:** Stack mein sirf top se add/remove hota hai. LIFO matlab aakhri add kiya hua pehle remove hota hai. Jaise kitaabon ka dher.

**4. Differentiate between the Enqueue and Dequeue operations of queue.**

**Answer:** Enqueue adds an item to the back of the queue. Dequeue removes an item from the front of the queue. Enqueue is like joining a line; dequeue is like being served.

**Roman Urdu:** Enqueue peeche add karta hai. Dequeue aage se remove karta hai. Enqueue line mein lagna, dequeue serve hona.

**5. Name two basic operations performed on stack.**

**Answer:** Push (add to top) and Pop (remove from top).

**Roman Urdu:** Push (top par add) aur Pop (top se remove).

**6. What is difference between enqueue() and dequeue().**

**Answer:** Enqueue adds to the back (FIFO add). Dequeue removes from the front (FIFO remove).

**Roman Urdu:** Enqueue peeche add, dequeue aage se remove.

---

### Long Questions

**1. Discuss the dynamic size property of lists in Python. How does this property make lists more flexible?**

**Answer:** Dynamic size means a list can grow or shrink during program execution. You can add items with `append()` or `insert()`, and remove them with `remove()` or `pop()`. The list automatically adjusts its memory. This flexibility allows lists to handle unknown amounts of data, making them suitable for dynamic applications like user input, real-time data, and changing collections. Unlike fixed-size arrays, lists do not require pre-defining size.

**Roman Urdu:** List ka size change ho sakta hai — items add ya remove kar sakte hain. List khud memory adjust karti hai. Isse unknown data handle karna asaan hota hai.

**2. Explain the operations on stack with real-life example and Python code.**

**Answer:** Stack operations are Push (add to top) and Pop (remove from top). Real-life example: A stack of plates in a cafeteria — you add a plate on top (push) and take a plate from the top (pop). Python code:

```python
stack = []
stack.append("Plate 1")  # Push
stack.append("Plate 2")  # Push
top = stack.pop()        # Pop — removes "Plate 2"
print(stack)             # ['Plate 1']
```

**Roman Urdu:** Stack mein push (top par add) aur pop (top se remove). Jaise plates ka dher — upar se rakho, upar se nikalo.

**3. Write a simple program to implement a queue (insertion and deletion).**

**Answer:**
```python
from queue import Queue

q = Queue()
q.put("Ahmed")      # Enqueue
q.put("Fatima")     # Enqueue

print(q.get())      # Dequeue — Ahmed
print(q.get())      # Dequeue — Fatima
```

**Roman Urdu:** Queue mein `put()` se enqueue, `get()` se dequeue.

**4. Define Tree and explain its properties.**

**Answer:** A tree is a hierarchical data structure with a root node and child nodes connected by edges. Properties: (1) Root — topmost node; (2) Nodes and Edges — elements connected by lines; (3) Leaf — node without children; (4) Height — longest path from root to leaf; (5) Balanced — left and right branches nearly equal height. Applications: file systems, organizational charts, decision trees.

**Roman Urdu:** Tree hierarchical structure hai — root, nodes, edges, leaves. Height longest path hai. File systems aur organization charts mein use hota hai.

**5. What is a graph? Explain differences between directed and undirected graphs.**

**Answer:** A graph is a collection of vertices connected by edges, representing networks. Directed graphs have one-way edges (arrows), like one-way streets. Undirected graphs have two-way edges (lines), like friendships. Directed graphs are used for asymmetric relationships; undirected for symmetric relationships.

**Roman Urdu:** Graph vertices aur edges ka network hai. Directed mein one-way edges (arrows), undirected mein two-way (lines). Directed one-way street jaisa, undirected dosti jaisa.

### Important Definitions

1. **List:** Ordered collection of items with index-based access.
2. **Stack:** LIFO data structure with push and pop.
3. **Queue:** FIFO data structure with enqueue and dequeue.
4. **LIFO:** Last-In, First-Out.
5. **FIFO:** First-In, First-Out.
6. **Tree:** Hierarchical data structure with root and child nodes.
7. **Root:** Topmost node of a tree.
8. **Leaf:** Node without children.
9. **Height:** Longest path from root to leaf.
10. **Balanced Tree:** Left and right branches nearly equal height.
11. **Graph:** Vertices connected by edges.
12. **Vertex:** Node in a graph.
13. **Edge:** Connection between vertices.
14. **Directed Graph:** One-way edges.
15. **Undirected Graph:** Two-way edges.
16. **Weighted Graph:** Edges have values.
17. **Degree:** Number of edges connected to a vertex.

### Important Differences

| Comparison | Key Difference |
|------------|----------------|
| Stack vs. Queue | LIFO vs. FIFO |
| Tree vs. Graph | Hierarchy vs. Network |
| Directed vs. Undirected | One-way vs. Two-way |
| Push vs. Pop | Add vs. Remove |
| Enqueue vs. Dequeue | Add back vs. Remove front |
| Root vs. Leaf | Top vs. Bottom |

# CHAPTER 5: DATA ANALYTICS

## A. Chapter Introduction

**Chapter Overview:** This chapter introduces data analytics — the process of examining data to find useful information, patterns, and trends. You will learn basic statistics (mean, median, mode, variance, standard deviation), how to collect and prepare data, how to build simple statistical models (linear regression, logistic regression, clustering), and how to visualize data using charts and graphs.

**Learning Objectives (from textbook SLOs):**

- Build basic statistical models for real-world problems and evaluate their performance
- Understand and explain the principles of experimental design in data science
- Explain types, uses, and methods of data visualizations
- Understand the benefits of visualizing data through descriptive statistics
- Create and interpret data visualizations using tools like MS Excel, Google Sheets, Python, Tableau, and Matplotlib

**Why This Chapter Is Important:** Data is everywhere — in business, healthcare, education, and sports. Data analytics helps us make sense of this data and make better decisions. From predicting sales to understanding student performance, data analytics is a powerful skill.

**Roman Urdu:** Data analytics data ko examine karke useful information, patterns, aur trends dhundhne ka process hai. Isse business, healthcare, education, aur sports mein better decisions liye jate hain. Aaj ke daur mein data har jagah hai — isse samajhna zaroori hai.

**Real-Life Applications:** Businesses predict sales, doctors analyze patient data, teachers track student performance, sports teams analyze player statistics, governments make policy decisions.

## B. Complete Topic-Wise Notes

### 5.1 Basic Statistical Concepts

**Definition:** Statistics is a branch of mathematics that helps us understand and analyze data by summarizing large sets of information in a simple way.

**Easy Explanation:** Statistics is like a summary of a large amount of data. Instead of looking at every single number, statistics gives you a few key numbers that describe the whole dataset.

**Roman Urdu:** Statistics mathematics ki branch hai jo data ko samajhne aur analyze karne mein madad karti hai. Bari data ko chhote summary mein convert karti hai — jaise average nikalna.

#### 5.1.1 Measures of Central Tendency

**Definition:** Measures of central tendency help identify the "center" or typical value in a dataset.

**Roman Urdu:** Central tendency ke measures dataset ka "center" ya typical value batate hain.

There are three main measures: **Mean, Median, Mode.**

##### Mean

**Definition:** The mean is the average of all numbers in a dataset. Add all numbers and divide by total count.

**Formula:**
\[
\text{Mean} = \frac{\text{Sum of all values}}{\text{Total number of values}}
\]

**Example:** 5 students scored 50, 60, 70, 80, 90.
\[
\text{Mean} = \frac{50 + 60 + 70 + 80 + 90}{5} = \frac{350}{5} = 70
\]

**Roman Urdu:** Mean average hota hai — sab numbers jama karke total se divide karein. 50+60+70+80+90 = 350 ÷ 5 = 70.

**Easy Explanation:** If you want to know the "typical" score, the mean tells you what everyone would get if all scores were equal.

##### Median

**Definition:** The median is the middle value when numbers are arranged in order. If even number of values, median is average of the two middle numbers.

**Example (Odd):** 50, 60, 70, 80, 90 → Middle = 70.

**Example (Even):** 50, 60, 70, 80 → Middle two = 60 and 70 → Median = (60 + 70) / 2 = 65.

**Roman Urdu:** Median beech wali value hai jab numbers order mein hon. Agar values even hon to do beech wale numbers ka average.

##### Mode

**Definition:** The mode is the number that appears most often.

**Example:** 50, 60, 70, 70, 90 → Mode = 70 (appears twice).

**Example (Multiple Modes):** 50, 60, 70, 70, 60, 90 → Modes = 60 and 70 (both appear twice).

**Roman Urdu:** Mode wo number hai jo sab se zyada baar aaye. Agar do numbers barabar baar aayein to dono mode hain.

**Comparison: Mean vs. Median vs. Mode**

| Measure | Definition | Best For | Roman Urdu |
|---------|-----------|----------|------------|
| Mean | Average | General data | Average |
| Median | Middle value | Data with outliers | Beech wali value |
| Mode | Most frequent | Categorical data | Sab se zyada baar |

---

#### 5.1.2 Measures of Dispersion

**Definition:** Measures of dispersion tell us how spread out or scattered the data is.

**Roman Urdu:** Dispersion ke measures batate hain ke data kitna phaila hua hai — numbers average se kitne door hain.

##### 5.1.2.1 Variance

**Definition:** Variance shows how much numbers differ from the mean. Higher variance = more spread out; lower variance = closer to mean.

**Formula:**
\[
\text{Variance } (\sigma^2) = \frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}(x_i - \mu)^2
\]

Where:
- \(x_i\) = each value
- \(\mu\) = mean
- \(N\) = total number of values

**Roman Urdu:** Variance batata hai ke numbers mean se kitne door hain. Zyada variance = zyada phaila hua. Formula mein har value ka mean se farq nikal kar square karte hain, phir average.

**Example: Class A vs. Class B**

**Class A:** 50, 52, 55, 57, 60
- Mean: (50+52+55+57+60)/5 = 274/5 = 54.8
- Deviations: -4.8, -2.8, 0.2, 2.2, 5.2
- Squared: 23.04, 7.84, 0.04, 4.84, 27.04
- Variance: (23.04 + 7.84 + 0.04 + 4.84 + 27.04)/5 = 62.8/5 = **12.56**

**Class B:** 30, 45, 55, 75, 90
- Mean: (30+45+55+75+90)/5 = 295/5 = 59
- Deviations: -29, -14, -4, 16, 31
- Squared: 841, 196, 16, 256, 961
- Variance: (841 + 196 + 16 + 256 + 961)/5 = 2270/5 = **454**

**Conclusion:** Class B has much higher variance (454 vs. 12.56), meaning its scores are more spread out.

**Roman Urdu:** Class A ka variance 12.56 hai, Class B ka 454. Class B ke numbers mean se bohat door hain — zyada spread out.

##### 5.1.2.2 Standard Deviation

**Definition:** Standard deviation is the square root of variance. It is in the same unit as the original data, making it easier to interpret.

**Formula:**
\[
\text{Standard Deviation } (\sigma) = \sqrt{\text{Variance}}
\]

**Example:**
- Class A: \(\sqrt{12.56} = 3.55\)
- Class B: \(\sqrt{454} = 21.31\)

**Interpretation:** Class A scores are closely packed around the mean (SD = 3.55). Class B scores are widely scattered (SD = 21.31).

**Roman Urdu:** Standard deviation variance ka square root hai. Class A ka SD 3.55 — numbers mean ke qareeb. Class B ka SD 21.31 — numbers mean se door.

**Comparison: Variance vs. Standard Deviation**

| Feature | Variance | Standard Deviation |
|---------|----------|-------------------|
| Definition | Average squared deviation | Square root of variance |
| Unit | Squared units | Same as original data |
| Interpretation | Less intuitive | More intuitive |
| Roman Urdu | Squared units mein | Original units mein |

---

#### 5.1.3 Introduction to Probability

**Definition:** Probability is the study of how likely an event is to happen.

**Formula:**
\[
\text{Probability} = \frac{\text{Number of favorable outcomes}}{\text{Total number of outcomes}}
\]

**Example: Coin flip**
- Possible outcomes: Heads, Tails
- Probability of heads = 1/2 = 50%
- Probability of tails = 1/2 = 50%

**Roman Urdu:** Probability batati hai ke koi cheez hone ka kitna chance hai. Coin mein heads ka chance 1/2 (50%).

**Applications:** Weather prediction, business decisions, sports outcomes, risk assessment.

**Textbook Tidbit:** "Statistics can help us understand patterns in data, leading to better decision-making in various fields, from healthcare to marketing!"

**Roman Urdu:** Statistics patterns samajhne mein madad karti hai — healthcare se marketing tak better decisions.

**Class Activity (Textbook):** Collect data from 10 classmates about homework hours, calculate mean, median, mode, variance, standard deviation, and reflect on study habits.

**Solution Example (Sample Data: 5, 8, 10, 12, 15, 6, 9, 11, 7, 13):**

**Data:** [5, 8, 10, 12, 15, 6, 9, 11, 7, 13]

**Mean:** Sum = 96, Mean = 96/10 = **9.6**

**Median:** Sorted: [5, 6, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 15]. Middle two = 9 and 10. Median = (9+10)/2 = **9.5**

**Mode:** All numbers appear once. **No mode.**

**Variance:**
- Mean = 9.6
- Deviations: -4.6, -1.6, 0.4, 2.4, 5.4, -3.6, -0.6, 1.4, -2.6, 3.4
- Squared: 21.16, 2.56, 0.16, 5.76, 29.16, 12.96, 0.36, 1.96, 6.76, 11.56
- Sum = 92.4
- Variance = 92.4/10 = **9.24**

**Standard Deviation:** \(\sqrt{9.24} =\) **3.04**

**Reflection:** Most students study around 9-10 hours per week. The data is moderately spread out (SD ≈ 3).

**Roman Urdu:** Mean 9.6, Median 9.5, Mode nahi. Variance 9.24, SD 3.04. Zyada tar students 9-10 ghante padhte hain.

---

### 5.2 Data Collection and Preparation

#### 5.2.1 Data Collection Methods

**Definition:** Data collection is the process of gathering relevant information for a particular purpose.

**Roman Urdu:** Data collection mein relevant information gather karte hain.

**Three Methods:**

##### 5.2.1.1 Surveys

**Definition:** Asking a predefined set of questions to a sample group. Can be online, phone, or in-person.

**Example (Textbook):** A grocery store survey asking:
1. Which product categories do you buy most often?
2. Are there products you'd like to see more often?
3. How often do you shop here?
4. What influences your purchasing decisions?
5. Any additional comments?

**Roman Urdu:** Surveys mein logon se sawal poochte hain — online, phone, ya face-to-face. Jaise grocery store ka survey.

**Advantages:** Large amounts of data, structured, easy to analyze.
**Disadvantages:** People may not answer honestly, low response rates.

##### 5.2.1.2 Observations

**Definition:** Collecting data by watching or monitoring subjects in their natural environment without interference.

**Example (Textbook):** A restaurant observes which tables customers choose during lunchtime over one week. Based on observations, they rearrange seating to optimize comfort and traffic flow.

**Roman Urdu:** Observation mein logon ko unke natural environment mein dekhte hain — bina interfere kiye. Jaise restaurant mein customers kis table par baithte hain.

**Advantages:** Real behavior, no self-report bias.
**Disadvantages:** Time-consuming, observer bias possible.

##### 5.2.1.3 Experiments

**Definition:** Manipulating one or more variables to determine their effect on another variable.

**Example (Textbook):** A teacher tests whether printed notes improve exam performance. Two groups: one gets printed notes, the other relies on lectures. After one month, both take the same test. Results are compared.

**Roman Urdu:** Experiment mein ek variable change karke doosre par asar dekhte hain. Jaise teacher printed notes dekar dekhta hai ke marks behtar hote hain ya nahi.

**Advantages:** Can establish cause-and-effect.
**Disadvantages:** Requires controlled environment, may not reflect real world.

**Comparison: Surveys vs. Observations vs. Experiments**

| Feature | Surveys | Observations | Experiments |
|---------|---------|--------------|-------------|
| Method | Ask questions | Watch behavior | Manipulate variables |
| Control | Low | Low | High |
| Cause-effect | No | No | Yes |
| Example | Questionnaire | Restaurant seating | Printed notes test |
| Roman Urdu | Sawal poochna | Dekhna | Variable change karna |

---

#### 5.2.2 Data Preparation

**Definition:** Once data is collected, it must be prepared — cleaned, organized, and formatted for analysis.

**Roman Urdu:** Data prepare karna matlab usse clean, organize, aur analyze ke liye ready karna.

**Steps:**
1. **Data Cleaning:** Remove errors, inconsistencies, duplicates
2. **Data Transformation:** Convert to suitable format, create new columns, reorganize
3. **Handling Missing Data:** Fill in or remove missing values

**Roman Urdu:** Data cleaning mein errors aur duplicates hataate hain. Transformation mein format change karte hain. Missing data ko fill ya remove karte hain.

**Example (Textbook):** Survey responses with incomplete information — missing values estimated based on available data.

---

#### 5.2.3 Data Cleaning and Transformation

##### 5.2.3.1 Data Cleaning

**Definition:** Correcting or removing problems in data — incorrect entries, missing values, duplicates.

**Example (Textbook Table 5.1):**

**Original Data (with errors):**

| Name | Scores | Class | Section |
|------|--------|-------|---------|
| Ali | 84 | 10 | A |
| Alie | 90 | 10 | A |
| Sara | (missing) | 10 | A |

**Cleaned Data (Table 5.2):**

| Name | Grade | Class | Section |
|------|-------|-------|---------|
| Ali | 84 | 10 | A |
| Alie | 90 | 10 | A |
| Sara | 87 | 10 | A |

**Explanation:** "Alie" corrected (or confirmed), Sara's missing score filled with 87 (class average).

**Roman Urdu:** Data cleaning mein ghalat naam theek kiya, missing score average se fill kiya.

##### 5.2.3.2 Data Transformation

**Definition:** Converting data into a format easier to work with — changing formats, creating new columns, aggregating.

**Example (Textbook):** Instead of individual student grades, aggregate to show class-level statistics (average, highest, lowest).

**Roman Urdu:** Data transformation mein data ko analyze ke liye behtar format mein badalte hain — jaise individual grades ki bajaye class average.

##### 5.2.3.3 Handling Missing Data

**Three Techniques:**

**1. Imputation:** Estimate missing value using existing data.
- Example: Sara's missing score filled with class average (87).

**Roman Urdu:** Imputation mein missing value ko estimate karte hain — jaise class average se.

**2. Flagging:** Keep track of missing data with a note, making analysts aware.

**Roman Urdu:** Flagging mein missing data ko note kar lete hain taake pata rahe.

**3. Removal:** Exclude rows with missing values if few and not significant.

**Roman Urdu:** Removal mein missing data wali row hata dete hain agar zyada important na ho.

**Comparison: Imputation vs. Flagging vs. Removal**

| Technique | Method | Advantage | Disadvantage | Roman Urdu |
|-----------|--------|-----------|--------------|------------|
| Imputation | Fill with estimate | Complete dataset | May introduce bias | Average se fill |
| Flagging | Mark as missing | Transparent | Incomplete data | Note kar lena |
| Removal | Delete row | Simple | Loses information | Row hata dena |

---

### 5.3 Building Statistical Models

#### 5.3.1 Introduction to Statistical Modeling

**Definition:** Statistical modeling uses data to make sense of the real world and predict future outcomes.

**Easy Explanation:** A statistical model is like a simplified map of reality. It helps you understand relationships and make predictions.

**Roman Urdu:** Statistical model reality ka simplified map hai — relationships samajhne aur predictions karne ke liye.

**Example:** Predicting grocery expenses based on past spending, family size, and income.

#### 5.3.1.1 Model Development

**Step 1: Define the Problem**
- Understand what you are trying to predict or explain.
- Example: Predict grocery expenses — factors like family size, location, income.

**Roman Urdu:** Pehle problem define karein — kya predict karna hai.

**Step 2: Collect Data**
- Gather relevant data.
- Example: Past spending habits, number of family members, income.

**Roman Urdu:** Relevant data gather karein.

**Step 3: Choose an Algorithm**
- Select appropriate method (linear regression, logistic regression, etc.).

**Roman Urdu:** Sahi algorithm chunein.

**Step 4: Train the Model**
- The model learns from the data.

**Roman Urdu:** Model data se seekhta hai.

**Step 5: Evaluate the Model**
- Test with new data to check accuracy.

**Roman Urdu:** Naye data se test karein.

##### 5.3.1.2 Linear Regression

**Definition:** Linear regression helps understand the relationship between two variables and predicts one based on the other.

**Easy Explanation:** Linear regression draws a straight line through data points to show the relationship. If one variable goes up, the other goes up (or down) predictably.

**Roman Urdu:** Linear regression do variables ke relationship ko ek seedhi line se dikhata hai. Ek barhe to doosra bhi barhta hai (ya kam hota hai).

**Formula:**
\[
Y = \beta_0 + \beta_1 X + \epsilon
\]

Where:
- \(Y\) = dependent variable (what we predict)
- \(X\) = independent variable (what we use to predict)
- \(\beta_0\) = intercept (value of Y when X = 0)
- \(\beta_1\) = slope (change in Y for each unit change in X)
- \(\epsilon\) = error term

**Textbook Example: Fruit Stall**

**Data (Table 5.3):**

| Customers (X) | Daily Earnings (Y) |
|---------------|-------------------|
| 10 | 500 |
| 15 | 700 |
| 20 | 900 |
| 25 | 1,100 |
| 30 | 1,300 |

**Step 1: Understanding the Slope (\(\beta_1\))**

For every 5 extra customers, earnings go up by 200 rupees.

\[
\beta_1 = \frac{200}{5} = 40
\]

**Meaning:** Each new customer adds 40 rupees to earnings.

**Roman Urdu:** Har 5 customers par 200 rupees zyada. Matlab har customer 40 rupees deta hai.

**Step 2: Understanding the Intercept (\(\beta_0\))**

Using the equation with a data point (10 customers, 500 rupees):
\[
500 = \beta_0 + 40 \times 10
\]
\[
500 = \beta_0 + 400
\]
\[
\beta_0 = 100
\]

**Meaning:** Even with zero customers, base earnings = 100 rupees.

**Roman Urdu:** Zero customers par bhi 100 rupees base earnings.

**Final Equation:**
\[
\text{Earnings} = 100 + 40 \times \text{Customers}
\]

**Step 3: Interpreting the Model**

If 22 customers expected:
\[
\text{Earnings} = 100 + 40 \times 22 = 100 + 880 = 980 \text{ Rs}
\]

**Step 4: Testing the Model**

Day 6: 28 customers, actual earnings = 1,250 Rs.
\[
\text{Predicted} = 100 + 40 \times 28 = 100 + 1,120 = 1,220 \text{ Rs}
\]
\[
\text{Error} = 1,220 - 1,250 = -30 \text{ Rs}
\]

**Conclusion:** Prediction was close but not perfect — real-world data has variation.

**Roman Urdu:** 28 customers par 1,220 rupees predict hua, actual 1,250. Error -30. Prediction qareeb thi lekin perfect nahi.

**Textbook Tidbit:** "To improve your statistical model, use more data points, include relevant factors, regularly update the model, and test predictions against actual spending."

**Roman Urdu:** Model behtar karne ke liye zyada data, relevant factors, regular updates, aur testing karein.

##### 5.3.1.3 Logistic Regression

**Definition:** Logistic regression predicts outcomes that can be categorized as "yes" or "no" (binary). It gives a probability between 0 and 1.

**Easy Explanation:** Instead of predicting an exact number, logistic regression predicts the chance of something happening. Like predicting whether a student will pass (yes/no) based on study hours.

**Roman Urdu:** Logistic regression "haan/nahi" wale outcomes predict karta hai — probability ke through (0 se 1). Jaise student pass hoga ya nahi.

**Example:** Predict if a student will pass an exam based on study hours.

**Comparison: Linear vs. Logistic Regression**

| Feature | Linear Regression | Logistic Regression |
|---------|-------------------|---------------------|
| Output | Continuous number | Probability (0-1) |
| Use Case | Predict amount | Predict yes/no |
| Example | Earnings prediction | Pass/fail prediction |
| Roman Urdu | Number predict | Haan/nahi predict |

##### 5.3.1.4 Clustering Techniques

**Definition:** Clustering groups similar things together based on characteristics.

**Easy Explanation:** Clustering is like sorting students into groups based on their strengths. Students good at math go in one group, students good at English in another.

**Roman Urdu:** Clustering mein similar cheezon ko groups mein divide karte hain. Jaise students ko unki strength ke hisaab se groups mein.

**Textbook Example: Clustering Students by Performance**

| Student | Math Score | English Score |
|---------|-----------|---------------|
| Basim | 85 | 70 |
| Umer | 90 | 65 |
| Anie | 50 | 80 |
| Tallat | 40 | 85 |
| Maliha | 60 | 60 |

**K-means Clustering:**
- Choose number of clusters (K). Here, K = 2.
- Algorithm groups students with similar scores.
- Cluster 1 (good at math): Basim, Umer
- Cluster 2 (good at English): Anie, Tallat
- Maliha (60, 60) may join either cluster based on distance.

**Roman Urdu:** K-means mein 2 clusters banaye. Math mein acha (Basim, Umer) ek cluster, English mein acha (Anie, Tallat) doosra.

**How K-means Works:**
1. Choose K (number of clusters)
2. Randomly initialize K centers
3. Assign each point to nearest center
4. Recalculate centers
5. Repeat until stable

**Roman Urdu:** K-means mein K clusters choose karte hain, centers banate hain, points assign karte hain, centers recalculate karte hain, repeat karte hain.

---

#### 5.3.2 Evaluating and Interpreting Models

##### 5.3.2.1 Performance Metrics

**Error Metrics:** Measure how much predictions differ from actual values.
- Example: Predicted grocery bill 8,000 Rs, actual 10,000 Rs → Error = 2,000 Rs.

**Roman Urdu:** Error metrics batate hain predictions actual se kitne different hain.

**Accuracy Metrics:** Measure how many predictions were correct (percentage).
- Example: Model predicts pass/fail — accuracy = correct predictions / total predictions.

**Roman Urdu:** Accuracy batati hai kitne predictions sahi the.

##### 5.3.2.2 Interpreting Outputs

**Drawing Conclusions:** If model shows study hours strongly affect scores, conclude that students should study more.

**Roman Urdu:** Model ke output se conclusions nikalte hain — jaise zyada padhne se marks behtar.

##### 5.3.2.3 Ethical Considerations

**Fairness and Bias:** Model should not unfairly favor one group.
- Example: Loan model should not discriminate based on gender.

**Roman Urdu:** Model fair hona chahiye — kisi group ko unfairly favor na kare.

**Data Privacy:** Personal data must be secure and not shared without permission.

**Roman Urdu:** Personal data secure rakhna zaroori hai.

**Textbook Tidbit:** "Always visualize your data before building models and test your results on new data for better accuracy."

**Roman Urdu:** Model banane se pehle data visualize karein aur naye data par test karein.

---

### 5.4 Introduction to Data Visualization

**Definition:** Data visualization represents data in visual format (graphs, charts) to quickly identify patterns, trends, and insights.

**Roman Urdu:** Data visualization data ko graphs aur charts mein dikhata hai taake patterns aur trends jaldi samajh aayein.

#### 5.4.1 Types of Visualizations

##### 5.4.1.1 Bar Charts

**Definition:** Compare different categories. Each bar represents a category; height shows value.

**Example (Figure 5.1):** Sales figures for different products — Product 1 and Product 2 compared across months.

**Roman Urdu:** Bar chart different categories compare karta hai. Har bar ki height value dikhati hai. Jaise different products ki sales.

**Figure 5.1 Explanation:** X-axis shows categories (months), Y-axis shows sales values. Bars of different heights for Product 1 and Product 2.

**When to Use:** Comparing categories (products, months, regions).

##### 5.4.1.2 Line Graphs

**Definition:** Show trends over time. Plot data points and connect with a line.

**Example (Figure 5.2):** Temperature variation over a day — shows rise and fall.

**Roman Urdu:** Line graph time ke saath trends dikhata hai. Jaise din mein temperature ka badalna.

**Figure 5.2 Explanation:** X-axis shows time (10am to 8pm), Y-axis shows temperature (°C). Line connects data points showing temperature changes.

**When to Use:** Showing trends over time (temperature, sales, stock prices).

##### 5.4.1.3 Histograms

**Definition:** Show distribution of data. Group data into bins/intervals to see frequency.

**Example (Figure 5.3):** Distribution of exam scores — how many students scored in each range.

**Roman Urdu:** Histogram data ka distribution dikhata hai — kitne students kis range mein aaye.

**Figure 5.3 Explanation:** X-axis shows score ranges (50-55, 55-60, etc.), Y-axis shows frequency (number of students). Bars show how many students in each range.

**When to Use:** Understanding data distribution (scores, ages, incomes).

##### 5.4.1.4 Scatterplots

**Definition:** Display relationship between two variables. Each point represents an observation.

**Example (Figure 5.4):** Relationship between hours studied and exam scores.

**Roman Urdu:** Scatterplot do variables ke relationship dikhata hai. Har point ek observation hai. Jaise padhai ke ghante aur marks.

**Figure 5.4 Explanation:** X-axis shows hours studied, Y-axis shows exam scores. Points show the relationship — more study hours generally mean higher scores.

**When to Use:** Exploring relationships between two variables.

##### 5.4.1.5 Boxplots

**Definition:** Summarize data distribution showing median, quartiles, and outliers.

**Example (Figure 5.5):** Compare exam scores of different classes.

**Roman Urdu:** Boxplot data ka summary dikhata hai — median, quartiles, outliers. Jaise different classes ke scores compare karna.

**Figure 5.5 Explanation:** Each box shows median (middle line), quartiles (box edges), and whiskers (range). Outliers shown as points.

**When to Use:** Comparing distributions across groups, identifying outliers.

**Comparison: Types of Visualizations**

| Type | Purpose | Example | Roman Urdu |
|------|---------|---------|------------|
| Bar Chart | Compare categories | Product sales | Categories compare |
| Line Graph | Show trends over time | Temperature | Time trends |
| Histogram | Show distribution | Exam scores | Distribution |
| Scatterplot | Show relationship | Study hours vs. scores | Relationship |
| Boxplot | Summarize distribution | Class comparison | Summary |

---

### 5.5 Tools for Data Visualization

#### 5.5.1 Using Excel and Google Sheets for Visualization

**Definition:** Excel and Google Sheets allow easy data entry and chart creation.

**Example (Textbook):** Track monthly product sales — enter data, create bar chart with a few clicks.

**Roman Urdu:** Excel aur Google Sheets mein data enter karke charts banate hain — jaise monthly sales ka bar chart.

#### 5.5.2 Creating and Interpreting Visualizations

**Step-by-Step Guide:**

1. **Enter Your Data:** Enter data in spreadsheet (e.g., months in one column, sales in another).
2. **Select the Data:** Highlight the data you want to visualize.
3. **Choose a Chart Type:** Click "Insert" tab, select chart type (bar, line, etc.).
4. **Customize the Chart:** Add labels (x-axis, y-axis), title, colors.
5. **Interpret:** Understand what the chart tells you — trends, patterns, outliers.

**Roman Urdu:** Data enter karein, select karein, chart type chunein, customize karein, aur interpret karein.

**Example:**
- Data: Months (Jan, Feb, Mar) and Sales (100, 150, 120)
- Select data → Insert → Bar Chart
- Chart shows February had highest sales

**Roman Urdu:** Jan 100, Feb 150, Mar 120. Bar chart se pata chalta hai February mein sab se zyada sales.

**Understanding Statistical Representations:**
- Look for trends (upward, downward)
- Compare categories
- Identify outliers
- Check distribution shape

**Roman Urdu:** Chart mein trends, comparisons, outliers, aur distribution dekhein.

| Variance | Average squared deviation | Squared deviation ka average |
| Standard Deviation | Square root of variance | Variance ka square root |
| Probability | Likelihood of event | Event ka chance |
| Survey | Asking questions | Sawal poochna |
| Observation | Watching behavior | Dekhna |
| Experiment | Manipulating variables | Variables change karna |
| Data Cleaning | Fixing errors in data | Data ke errors theek karna |
| Data Transformation | Changing data format | Data ka format change |
| Imputation | Filling missing values | Missing values bharna |
| Linear Regression | Predicting numbers | Numbers predict karna |
| Logistic Regression | Predicting yes/no | Haan/nahi predict |
| Clustering | Grouping similar items | Similar cheezein group karna |
| K-means | Clustering algorithm | Clustering algorithm |
| Bar Chart | Compare categories | Categories compare |
| Line Graph | Show trends over time | Time trends |

| Outlier | Unusual value | Ajeeb value |

## D. Differences and Comparisons

### Mean vs. Median vs. Mode

| Feature | Mean | Median | Mode |
|---------|------|--------|------|
| Definition | Average | Middle value | Most frequent |
| Affected by outliers | Yes | No | No |
| Best for | General data | Skewed data | Categorical data |
| Example | (50+60+70)/3=60 | 60 | 60 (if appears most) |
| Roman Urdu | Average | Beech wali | Sab se zyada baar |

### Variance vs. Standard Deviation

| Feature | Variance | Standard Deviation |
|---------|----------|-------------------|
| Definition | Average squared deviation | Square root of variance |
| Unit | Squared | Original |
| Interpretation | Less intuitive | More intuitive |
| Roman Urdu | Squared units | Original units |

### Linear vs. Logistic Regression

| Feature | Linear Regression | Logistic Regression |
|---------|-------------------|---------------------|
| Output | Continuous number | Probability (0-1) |
| Use Case | Predict amount | Predict yes/no |
| Example | Earnings | Pass/fail |
| Roman Urdu | Number predict | Haan/nahi predict |

### Surveys vs. Observations vs. Experiments

| Feature | Surveys | Observations | Experiments |
|---------|---------|--------------|-------------|
| Method | Ask questions | Watch behavior | Manipulate variables |
| Control | Low | Low | High |
| Cause-effect | No | No | Yes |
| Roman Urdu | Sawal | Dekhna | Variable change |

### Bar Chart vs. Line Graph vs. Histogram vs. Scatterplot

| Type | Purpose | X-axis | Y-axis |
|------|---------|--------|--------|
| Bar Chart | Compare categories | Categories | Values |
| Line Graph | Show trends | Time | Values |
| Histogram | Show distribution | Ranges | Frequency |
| Scatterplot | Show relationship | Variable 1 | Variable 2 |
| Roman Urdu | Categories compare | Time trends | Distribution | Relationship |

---

## F. Python Programming

*Chapter 5 does not contain Python programs in the textbook, but here are implementations for key concepts:*

### Program 1: Mean, Median, Mode

```python
import statistics

data = [50, 60, 70, 80, 90]

mean = statistics.mean(data)
median = statistics.median(data)
mode = statistics.mode(data) if len(set(data)) < len(data) else "No mode"

print("Mean:", mean)
print("Median:", median)
print("Mode:", mode)
```

**Output:**
```
Mean: 70
Median: 70
Mode: No mode
```

**Roman Urdu:** Statistics library se mean, median, mode nikalte hain.

### Program 2: Variance and Standard Deviation

```python
import statistics

data = [50, 52, 55, 57, 60]

variance = statistics.variance(data)
std_dev = statistics.stdev(data)

print("Variance:", variance)
print("Standard Deviation:", std_dev)
```

**Output:**
```
Variance: 15.7
Standard Deviation: 3.96
```

**Roman Urdu:** Variance aur standard deviation statistics library se.

---

## G. Important Formulas and Rules

### Mean
\[
\text{Mean} = \frac{\sum x_i}{N}
\]

### Variance
\[
\sigma^2 = \frac{\sum (x_i - \mu)^2}{N}
\]

### Standard Deviation
\[
\sigma = \sqrt{\sigma^2}
\]

### Probability
\[
P = \frac{\text{Favorable outcomes}}{\text{Total outcomes}}
\]

### Linear Regression
\[
Y = \beta_0 + \beta_1 X
\]
- \(\beta_0\) = intercept
- \(\beta_1\) = slope

**Roman Urdu:** Mean = sum/N. Variance = squared deviations ka average. SD = variance ka square root. Probability = favorable/total. Regression = intercept + slope × X.

**1. An example of a basic statistical model:**
a) Linear Regression
b) Neural Networks
c) Decision Trees
d) Support Vector Machines

**Roman Urdu:** Linear regression basic statistical model hai.

**2. The activity involved in experimental design in data science:**
a) Creating visualizations
b) Collecting and analyzing data systematically
c) Writing code for machine learning
d) Building databases

**Answer: b) Collecting and analyzing data systematically**
**Explanation:** Experimental design involves systematic data collection and analysis.
**Roman Urdu:** Experimental design mein systematic data collection aur analysis.

**3. A commonly used tool for creating data visualizations:**
a) MS Excel
b) Python (Matplotlib)
c) Tableau
d) All of the above

**Answer: d) All of the above**
**Explanation:** All are valid data visualization tools.
**Roman Urdu:** Sab tools data visualization ke liye use hote hain.

**4. The meaning of the slope in a linear regression model:**
a) The intercept of the model
b) The change in the dependent variable for a unit change in the independent variable
c) The error term
d) The mean of the data

**Answer: b) The change in the dependent variable for a unit change in the independent variable**
**Explanation:** Slope shows how much Y changes when X increases by 1.
**Roman Urdu:** Slope batata hai X mein 1 ka change aane par Y mein kitna change hota hai.

**5. An example of a real-world application of statistical models:**
a) Predicting house prices
b) Creating social media posts
c) Designing websites
d) Writing essays

**Answer: a) Predicting house prices**
**Explanation:** Statistical models are used for prediction, like house prices.
**Roman Urdu:** Statistical models se house prices predict karte hain.

**6. Option not considered a benefit of data visualization:**
a) Identifying trends and patterns
b) Communicating insights effectively
c) Making data more complex
d) Summarizing large datasets

**Answer: c) Making data more complex**
**Explanation:** Visualization simplifies data, not complicates it.
**Roman Urdu:** Visualization data ko simple banata hai, complex nahi.

**7. A primary goal of K-Means Clustering:**
a) To classify data into predefined categories
b) To group data into clusters based on similarity
c) To predict continuous outcomes
d) To reduce the dimensionality of data

**Answer: b) To group data into clusters based on similarity**
**Explanation:** K-means groups similar data points into clusters.
**Roman Urdu:** K-means similar data ko clusters mein group karta hai.

**8. The meaning of "K" in K-Means Clustering:**
a) Number of features in the dataset
b) Number of clusters to be formed
c) Number of iterations required for convergence
d) Number of data points in the dataset

**Answer: b) Number of clusters to be formed**
**Explanation:** K represents the number of clusters.
**Roman Urdu:** K clusters ki tadaad hai.

### Short Questions

**1. What is the importance of building statistical models in real-world applications?**

**Answer:** Statistical models help understand relationships, predict future outcomes, and make informed decisions. They are used in business (sales prediction), healthcare (disease risk), and education (student performance).

**Roman Urdu:** Statistical models relationships samajhne, predictions karne, aur better decisions lene mein madad karte hain.

**2. Name one basic statistical model used for predicting outcomes and explain its purpose.**

**Answer:** Linear regression predicts a continuous outcome (e.g., earnings) based on an independent variable (e.g., customers). It finds the best-fit line showing the relationship.

**Roman Urdu:** Linear regression continuous outcome predict karta hai — jaise customers se earnings.

**3. List two types of data visualizations and describe when you would use each.**

**Answer:** (1) Bar chart — compare categories (e.g., product sales). (2) Line graph — show trends over time (e.g., temperature changes).

**Roman Urdu:** Bar chart categories compare karne ke liye. Line graph time trends ke liye.

**4. How does visualizing data help in understanding descriptive statistics?**

**Answer:** Visualization shows distribution, trends, and outliers visually. Mean, median, and spread become easier to understand when seen in a chart (e.g., histogram shows distribution shape).

**Roman Urdu:** Visualization se distribution, trends, outliers dikhte hain. Mean, median, spread charts mein asaan samajh aate hain.

---

### Long Questions

**1. Explain the role and importance of statistical models in solving real-world problems.**

**Answer:** Statistical models simplify complex real-world data into understandable relationships. They help predict outcomes (sales, disease risk), identify patterns (customer behavior), and support decision-making (policy, business strategy). Models like linear regression, logistic regression, and clustering are used in healthcare, finance, marketing, and education. They provide a systematic way to turn data into actionable insights.

**Roman Urdu:** Statistical models complex data ko simple relationships mein convert karte hain. Predictions, patterns, aur decisions mein madad karte hain. Linear regression, logistic regression, clustering healthcare, finance, marketing, education mein use hote hain.

**2. Describe the steps involved in building a basic statistical model (e.g., linear regression). Include details on data collection, model training, and evaluation.**

**Answer:** Steps: (1) Define problem — what to predict. (2) Collect data — relevant historical data. (3) Choose algorithm — e.g., linear regression. (4) Train model — fit line to data. (5) Evaluate — test on new data, calculate error. Example: Predicting fruit stall earnings from customers. Collect data (customers, earnings), find slope (40) and intercept (100), equation: Earnings = 100 + 40 × Customers. Test with new day data, calculate error.

**Roman Urdu:** Problem define, data collect, algorithm choose, model train, evaluate. Jaise fruit stall — customers se earnings predict.

**3. Discuss the types of data visualizations and their uses.**

**Answer:** (1) Bar chart — compare categories. (2) Line graph — trends over time. (3) Histogram — data distribution. (4) Scatterplot — relationship between two variables. (5) Boxplot — summarize distribution, identify outliers. Each type serves a specific purpose; choosing the right one depends on the data and the insight needed.

**Roman Urdu:** Bar chart categories compare, line graph trends, histogram distribution, scatterplot relationship, boxplot summary. Har type ka apna purpose hai.

**4. Explain data collection methods.**

**Answer:** (1) Surveys — ask predefined questions (online, phone, in-person) to gather large data. (2) Observations — watch behavior in natural environment without interference. (3) Experiments — manipulate variables to determine cause-effect. Each method has strengths: surveys for large data, observations for real behavior, experiments for causation.

**Roman Urdu:** Surveys mein sawal, observations mein dekhna, experiments mein variables change. Har method ka apna faida hai.

**5. Discuss the concept of measure of tendency with example.**

**Answer:** Measures of central tendency (mean, median, mode) describe the "center" of data. Mean = average (50+60+70+80+90)/5 = 70. Median = middle value = 70. Mode = most frequent (70 if it appears most). These measures summarize data with a single representative value.

**Roman Urdu:** Mean, median, mode central tendency ke measures hain. Mean average, median beech wali, mode sab se zyada baar. Ye data ko ek representative value se summarize karte hain.

### Important Definitions

1. **Statistics:** Branch of math for analyzing data.
2. **Mean:** Average of values.
3. **Median:** Middle value in ordered data.
4. **Mode:** Most frequent value.
5. **Variance:** Average squared deviation from mean.
6. **Standard Deviation:** Square root of variance.
7. **Probability:** Likelihood of an event.
8. **Survey:** Asking predefined questions.
9. **Observation:** Watching behavior.
10. **Experiment:** Manipulating variables.
11. **Data Cleaning:** Fixing errors in data.
12. **Data Transformation:** Changing data format.
13. **Imputation:** Filling missing values.
14. **Linear Regression:** Predicting continuous outcome.
15. **Logistic Regression:** Predicting binary outcome.
16. **Clustering:** Grouping similar items.
17. **K-means:** Clustering algorithm with K clusters.
18. **Bar Chart:** Comparing categories.
19. **Line Graph:** Showing trends over time.
20. **Histogram:** Showing distribution.
21. **Scatterplot:** Showing relationship between two variables.
22. **Boxplot:** Summarizing distribution.
23. **Outlier:** Unusual value far from others.
