# CHAPTER 6: EMERGING TECHNOLOGIES

## A. Chapter Introduction

**Chapter Overview:** This chapter introduces two transformative technologies: **Cloud Computing** and **Blockchain**. You will learn how cloud computing provides on-demand access to computing resources over the internet, and how blockchain creates secure, transparent, and decentralized records. These technologies are reshaping how businesses, governments, and individuals use and trust digital systems.

**Learning Objectives (from textbook SLOs):**
- Understand basic concepts of cloud computing (virtualization, scalability, on-demand access)
- Identify and explain types of cloud services (IaaS, PaaS, SaaS)
- Describe cloud deployment models (public, private, hybrid, multi-cloud)
- Recognize core principles of blockchain and role of peer-to-peer networks
- Explain applications of blockchain (cryptocurrencies, smart contracts, product tracking)
- Discuss implications of cloud computing and blockchain (data security, resource management)
- Explore future trends (edge computing, Blockchain 2.0)

**Why This Chapter Is Important:** Cloud computing powers most modern apps and services (Google Drive, Netflix, WhatsApp). Blockchain is the technology behind Bitcoin and is transforming finance, supply chains, and digital identity. Understanding these technologies prepares you for the digital future.

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud computing internet ke zariye computing resources provide karta hai — jaise Google Drive, Netflix. Blockchain secure aur transparent records banata hai — jaise Bitcoin. Yeh dono technologies digital duniya ko badal rahi hain.

**Real-Life Applications:** Cloud storage (Google Drive, Dropbox), streaming (Netflix, YouTube), cryptocurrency (Bitcoin), supply chain tracking (food safety), digital identity (secure login).

---

## B. Complete Topic-Wise Notes

### 6.1 Definition and Overview of Emerging Technologies

**Definition:** Emerging technologies are new tools, systems, and methods currently being developed or recently started being used. They have the potential to change how we live, work, and interact.

**Roman Urdu:** Emerging technologies naye tools, systems, aur methods hain jo abhi develop ho rahe hain ya haal hi mein use hona shuru hue hain. Yeh hamari zindagi badal sakte hain.

**Key Emerging Technologies (from textbook):**

**1. Artificial Intelligence (AI):**
- Machines and software that can learn and perform like humans
- Used in smart assistants (Siri), self-driving cars, face recognition
- Roman Urdu: AI machines ko insaan jaisa kaam karne ki salahiyat deta hai — jaise Siri, self-driving cars.

**2. Cloud Computing:**
- Store and access data and applications over the internet
- Examples: Google Drive, Dropbox, Amazon Web Services (AWS)
- Roman Urdu: Cloud computing internet ke zariye data aur apps store/access karta hai — jaise Google Drive, Dropbox.

**3. Blockchain:**
- Secure way to record and share information across many computers
- Best known for cryptocurrencies (Bitcoin)
- Also used in supply chains and contracts
- Roman Urdu: Blockchain secure tareeqa hai information record aur share karne ka — jaise Bitcoin.

**4. Internet of Things (IoT):**
- Connects everyday objects (refrigerators, cars, clothes) to the internet
- Allows them to send and receive data
- Example: Smart thermostat adjusts temperature automatically
- Roman Urdu: IoT rozmarra ki cheezon ko internet se jodta hai — jaise smart thermostat.

**5. Augmented Reality (AR) and Virtual Reality (VR):**
- AR adds digital elements to real world (smartphones, glasses)
- VR creates completely virtual environment
- Used in gaming, education, training
- Roman Urdu: AR real duniya mein digital cheezein add karta hai. VR puri virtual duniya banata hai. Gaming, education, training mein use.

**6. 5G Technology:**
- Next generation wireless technology
- Much faster internet speeds, more reliable connections
- Enables better performance for phones, smart devices, AR/VR
- Roman Urdu: 5G naye daur ki wireless technology hai — tez internet, reliable connections.

**7. Quantum Computing:**
- Uses qubits (can be 0 and 1 at same time)
- Solves problems much faster than normal computers
- Roman Urdu: Quantum computing qubits use karta hai — 0 aur 1 dono ek saath. Normal computers se bohat tez.

---

### 6.2 Cloud Computing

**Definition:** Cloud computing is a model that allows easy and convenient access to computing resources (servers, storage, applications) over the internet. These resources can be quickly provided and released with minimal management effort.

**Easy Explanation:** Cloud computing is like renting a powerful computer over the internet. Instead of buying your own servers, you use someone else's (like Google, Amazon) and pay only for what you use.

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud computing internet ke zariye computing resources (servers, storage, apps) access karne ka model hai. Jaise apna computer kharidne ki bajaye internet se rent par lena — jaise Amazon, Google se.

**Key Idea:** Like renting a supercomputer — use whenever needed, from anywhere, pay for what you use.

**Roman Urdu:** Supercomputer rent par lene jaisa — jab zaroorat ho use karein, jahan se chahein, jitna use karein utna pay karein.

#### 6.2.1 Basic Concepts of Cloud Computing

##### 6.2.1.1 Virtualization

**Definition:** Virtualization allows a single physical machine to run multiple virtual machines. Each virtual machine can run its own operating system and applications independently.

**Easy Explanation:** Virtualization is like dividing one large house into several apartments. Each apartment has its own kitchen, bathroom, and living space, even though the building is shared.

**Roman Urdu:** Virtualization mein ek physical computer ko multiple virtual computers mein divide karte hain. Jaise ek bade ghar ko kai apartments mein todna — har apartment apna kitchen, bathroom, living space.

**Benefits:**
- Better resource utilization
- Cost savings
- Isolation between virtual machines

**Roman Urdu:** Virtualization se resources behtar use hote hain, kharcha bachta hai, aur virtual machines alag alag rehti hain.

##### 6.2.1.2 Scalability and Elasticity

**Scalability:** Ability to add more resources when needed.
- Example: Online store during Eid sale — add more servers to handle increased traffic.

**Roman Urdu:** Scalability mein zaroorat par resources barha sakte hain. Jaise Eid sale mein zyada servers add karna.

**Elasticity:** Ability to automatically scale resources up or down based on demand.
- Example: E-commerce website automatically allocates more servers during sale, scales back after.

**Roman Urdu:** Elasticity mein resources automatically barhte ya ghatte hain demand ke hisaab se. Jaise sale ke waqt zyada servers, sale ke baad kam.

**Comparison: Scalability vs. Elasticity**

| Feature | Scalability | Elasticity |
|---------|-------------|------------|
| Definition | Add resources when needed | Automatic scaling up/down |
| Control | Manual or planned | Automatic |
| Example | Add servers for sale | Auto-scale during demand |
| Roman Urdu | Zaroorat par barhana | Automatically barhna/ghatna |

##### 6.2.1.3 On-Demand Access

**Definition:** Use computing resources whenever needed without waiting for a long setup process.

**Easy Explanation:** On-demand access is like turning on a tap to get water — instant availability. You don't need to dig a well first.

**Roman Urdu:** On-demand access matlab jab zaroorat ho foran resources mil jayein — jaise nal kholne par pani. Pehle kuan khodne ki zaroorat nahi.

**Example:** Need extra storage for a school project — instantly rent from cloud provider.

**Roman Urdu:** School project ke liye extra storage chahiye — foran cloud se rent par le lein.

---

#### 6.2.2 Types of Cloud Services

**Figure 6.1: Types of Cloud Services** — Shows IaaS (infrastructure), PaaS (platform), SaaS (software) layers.

**Roman Urdu:** Figure 6.1 mein cloud services ki layers dikhayi hain — IaaS (infrastructure), PaaS (platform), SaaS (software).

##### 6.2.2.1 Infrastructure as a Service (IaaS)

**Definition:** Offers basic computing infrastructure (servers, storage, networking) on a pay-as-you-go basis. Users control operating systems, applications, and storage, but not physical infrastructure.

**Easy Explanation:** IaaS is like renting an empty apartment. You get the space (servers, storage), but you furnish it yourself (install OS, apps).

**Roman Urdu:** IaaS mein basic infrastructure rent par milta hai — servers, storage, networking. Aap OS aur apps khud manage karte hain. Jaise khali apartment rent par lena.

**Examples:** Amazon Web Services (AWS), Microsoft Azure, Google Compute Engine.

**Roman Urdu:** AWS, Microsoft Azure, Google Compute Engine IaaS examples hain.

##### 6.2.2.2 Platform as a Service (PaaS)

**Definition:** Offers a complete development and deployment environment in the cloud, including infrastructure, middleware, development tools, and management services. Developers focus on coding without managing hardware/software layers.

**Easy Explanation:** PaaS is like renting a furnished apartment with kitchen and appliances. You just bring your food (code) and cook.

**Roman Urdu:** PaaS mein development environment ready milta hai — infrastructure, tools, sab kuch. Aap sirf code likhte hain. Jaise furnished apartment — bas khana la kar pakayein.

**Examples:** Google App Engine, Microsoft Azure App Services, Heroku.

**Roman Urdu:** Google App Engine, Azure App Services, Heroku PaaS examples hain.

##### 6.2.2.3 Software as a Service (SaaS)

**Definition:** Provides access to software applications hosted and managed by the service provider. Users subscribe and use over the internet. No hardware management or software updates needed.

**Easy Explanation:** SaaS is like eating at a restaurant. You don't cook or clean — you just enjoy the meal. The restaurant (provider) handles everything.

**Roman Urdu:** SaaS mein software ready-to-use milta hai — provider sab manage karta hai. Jaise restaurant mein khana — aapko pakana nahi, bas khana hai.

**Examples:** Google Workspace (Gmail, Google Docs, Google Drive), Microsoft Office 365, Salesforce.

**Roman Urdu:** Gmail, Google Docs, Office 365, Salesforce SaaS examples hain.

**Comparison: IaaS vs. PaaS vs. SaaS**

| Feature | IaaS | PaaS | SaaS |
|---------|------|------|------|
| What you get | Infrastructure (servers, storage) | Platform (development environment) | Software (ready-to-use app) |
| You manage | OS, apps, data | Apps, data | Nothing (just use) |
| Provider manages | Physical infrastructure | Infrastructure, platform | Everything |
| Example | AWS, Azure | Google App Engine | Gmail, Office 365 |
| Analogy | Empty apartment | Furnished apartment | Restaurant meal |
| Roman Urdu | Khali apartment | Furnished apartment | Restaurant ka khana |

---

#### 6.2.3 Cloud Deployment Models

##### 6.2.3.1 Public Cloud

**Definition:** Cloud service offered over the internet, shared among multiple organizations, managed by third-party provider.

**Example:** Amazon Web Services (AWS) — businesses of all sizes use it.

**Roman Urdu:** Public cloud internet par multiple organizations ke liye hota hai, third-party manage karta hai. Jaise AWS.

**Advantages:** Cost-effective, scalable, no maintenance.
**Disadvantages:** Less control, security concerns.

##### 6.2.3.2 Private Cloud

**Definition:** Cloud environment used exclusively by one organization. Can be hosted on-premises or by third-party, but not shared.

**Example:** A large bank uses private cloud for sensitive customer data.

**Roman Urdu:** Private cloud sirf ek organization ke liye hota hai — share nahi hota. Jaise bank apne sensitive data ke liye.

**Advantages:** High security, control, customization.
**Disadvantages:** Expensive, requires maintenance.

##### 6.2.3.3 Hybrid Cloud

**Definition:** Combines public and private clouds, allowing data and applications to be shared between them.

**Example:** Company uses public cloud for everyday operations and private cloud for sensitive data. During busy periods, moves less sensitive data to public cloud.

**Roman Urdu:** Hybrid cloud public aur private ka combination hai. Rozmarra kaam public cloud, sensitive data private cloud. Busy time mein data public cloud par shift.

**Advantages:** Flexibility, cost-effective, secure for sensitive data.
**Disadvantages:** Complex to manage.

##### 6.2.3.4 Multi-cloud

**Definition:** Using services from multiple cloud providers simultaneously (e.g., AWS, Azure, Google Cloud) to meet different needs.

**Example:** Global retail company uses AWS for e-commerce, Azure for internal apps, Google Cloud for data analytics.

**Roman Urdu:** Multi-cloud mein ek organization multiple providers use karta hai — AWS, Azure, Google Cloud — different needs ke liye. Jaise retail company — AWS e-commerce, Azure internal apps, Google Cloud analytics.

**Advantages:** Avoids vendor lock-in, resilience, best-of-breed services.
**Disadvantages:** Complex management, integration challenges.

**Comparison: Deployment Models**

| Model | Description | Example | Roman Urdu |
|-------|-------------|---------|------------|
| Public | Shared, third-party managed | AWS | Shared, third-party |
| Private | Exclusive, single organization | Bank's data center | Sirf ek organization |
| Hybrid | Public + private | Sensitive + everyday | Dono ka mix |
| Multi-cloud | Multiple providers | AWS + Azure + GCP | Multiple providers |

**Textbook Comparison:** "Public clouds are cost-effective but less secure. Private clouds are more secure but expensive. Hybrid clouds offer flexibility, while multi-clouds provide resilience."

**Roman Urdu:** Public sasta lekin kam secure. Private zyada secure lekin mehnga. Hybrid flexible. Multi-cloud resilient.

---

### 6.3 Applications and Implications of Cloud Computing

#### 6.3.1 Applications of Cloud Computing

##### 6.3.1.1 Data Storage

**Definition:** Save data on remote servers instead of local devices.

**Examples:** Google Drive, Dropbox — store and share files online. Businesses use cloud storage for backups.

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud storage mein data remote servers par save hota hai — jaise Google Drive, Dropbox. Businesses backup ke liye use karte hain.

##### 6.3.1.2 Web Hosting and Content Delivery

**Definition:** Infrastructure to host websites and deliver content efficiently.

**Examples:** AWS, Microsoft Azure — web hosting. Cloudflare — content delivery network (CDN) caches content close to users.

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud web hosting ke liye infrastructure deta hai — jaise AWS, Azure. CDN content ko users ke qareeb cache karta hai.

##### 6.3.1.3 Machine Learning and AI in the Cloud

**Definition:** Cloud platforms provide tools for developing and running ML/AI models.

**Examples:** Google Cloud AI, AWS SageMaker — build, train, deploy ML models without extensive local computing.

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud mein ML/AI tools milte hain — jaise Google Cloud AI, AWS SageMaker. Bina local computing ke models bana sakte hain.

#### 6.3.2 Implications of Cloud Computing

##### 6.3.2.1 Data Security

**Challenge:** Storing sensitive data on remote servers introduces risks (breaches, unauthorized access, data loss).

**Measures:** Encryption, strong authentication, regular security policy reviews.

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud mein sensitive data store karne se risks hain — breaches, unauthorized access. Encryption, strong authentication, security reviews se bachao.

##### 6.3.2.2 Scalability and Resource Management

**Challenge:** Effective resource management to avoid unnecessary costs and ensure performance.

**Measures:** Monitor usage, optimize resource allocation.

**Roman Urdu:** Resources ko manage karna zaroori hai taake kharcha zyada na ho aur performance behtar rahe.

##### 6.3.2.3 Cost Considerations

**Challenge:** Costs can add up if not monitored.

**Measures:** Regularly review usage and spending, optimize allocation, choose right pricing plans.

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud ka kharcha control karna zaroori hai — usage review karein, sahi plan chunein.

##### 6.3.2.4 Compliance and Regulatory Issues

**Challenge:** Must comply with legal and regulatory requirements (data privacy, security, industry standards).

**Measures:** Cloud providers offer tools to help meet compliance.

**Roman Urdu:** Legal aur regulatory requirements follow karna zaroori hai — data privacy, security standards. Providers tools dete hain.

**Class Activity (Textbook):** List cloud-based services you use. Describe benefits and security measures.

**Roman Urdu:** Apne cloud services list karein — faide aur security measures likhein.

---

### 6.4 Introduction to Blockchain Technology

**Definition:** Blockchain is a decentralized, distributed ledger that securely and transparently records transactions across many computers.

**Easy Explanation:** Blockchain is like a digital notebook shared with everyone in a group. Every change is recorded in all copies at the same time. No single person controls it.

**Roman Urdu:** Blockchain ek digital notebook jaisa hai jo group ke sab logon ke paas hota hai. Har change sab copies mein ek saath record hota hai. Koi ek person control nahi karta.

**Figure 6.2: Architecture of Blockchain** — Shows blocks connected in a chain, each block containing transactions, hash, and previous hash.

**Roman Urdu:** Figure 6.2 mein blocks ki chain dikhayi hai — har block mein transactions, hash, aur previous block ka hash.

#### 6.4.1 Fundamentals of Blockchain

**Core Features:**
- **Transparency:** Everyone can see what's written
- **Security:** Cryptography locks information — almost impossible to change
- **Decentralization:** No single person/computer in charge — everyone has equal copy

**Roman Urdu:** Transparency — sab dekh sakte hain. Security — cryptography se locked, change karna mushkil. Decentralization — koi ek in-charge nahi, sab ke paas copy.

##### 6.4.1.1 Core Principles

**1. Decentralization:**
- Maintained by a network of computers (nodes)
- No central authority
- Reduces single point of failure

**Roman Urdu:** Decentralization mein network ke computers (nodes) maintain karte hain — koi central authority nahi. Single point of failure kam.

**2. Immutability:**
- Once a block is added, it cannot be altered or deleted
- Transaction history is permanent and tamper-proof

**Roman Urdu:** Immutability matlab ek baar block add ho jaye to change ya delete nahi ho sakta. History permanent aur tamper-proof.

**3. Consensus Mechanisms:**
- Networks use consensus to agree on transaction validity
- All nodes reach unanimous decision before adding new block

**Roman Urdu:** Consensus mechanisms se sab nodes ek saath decide karte hain ke transaction valid hai ya nahi — naya block add karne se pehle.

##### 6.4.1.2 Blockchain Components

**1. Node:**
- A computer participating in the blockchain network
- Maintains copy of blockchain, validates transactions

**Roman Urdu:** Node wo computer hai jo blockchain network mein participate karta hai — copy rakhta hai, transactions validate karta hai.

**2. Ledger:**
- Shared digital record of all transactions

**Roman Urdu:** Ledger sab transactions ka shared digital record hai.

**3. Block:**
- Collection of transactions bundled together
- Contains unique identifier (hash), reference to previous block (parent hash), list of transactions

**Roman Urdu:** Block transactions ka collection hai — unique hash, previous block ka reference, aur transactions ki list.

**4. Transaction:**
- Individual entry representing transfer of assets or information

**Roman Urdu:** Transaction ek individual entry hai — assets ya information ka transfer.

**5. Blockchain Protocol:**
- Rules and procedures for validating transactions, adding blocks, achieving consensus

**Roman Urdu:** Protocol rules aur procedures hain — transactions validate karna, blocks add karna, consensus achieve karna.

##### 6.4.1.3 Peer-to-Peer Network and Its Usage in Blockchain

**Definition:** A P2P network is a system where computers (nodes) communicate and share resources directly without a central server. Each node acts as both client and server.

**Easy Explanation:** In a P2P network, computers talk directly to each other — no boss server. Like friends sharing files directly instead of through one central computer.

**Roman Urdu:** P2P network mein computers direct ek doosre se baat karte hain — koi central server nahi. Jaise dost direct files share karein, kisi central computer se nahi.

**Example:** File-sharing network — users download files directly from each other's computers.

**Roman Urdu:** File-sharing mein users ek doosre ke computers se direct files download karte hain.

**Role in Blockchain:** Blockchain uses P2P networks to share transactions and blocks among all nodes without a central authority.

**Roman Urdu:** Blockchain P2P network use karta hai — transactions aur blocks sab nodes ke beech share hote hain, bina central authority.

#### 6.4.2 Use Cases of Blockchain Technology

**1. Cryptocurrencies:**
- Bitcoin, Ethereum use blockchain for secure, decentralized transactions
- No intermediaries (banks) needed

**Roman Urdu:** Bitcoin, Ethereum blockchain par chalti hain — secure, decentralized transactions, bina bank ke.

**2. Supply Chain Management:**
- Track and verify movement of goods
- Prevent fraud, reduce errors, ensure authenticity

**Roman Urdu:** Supply chain mein goods ki movement track karna — fraud rokna, errors kam karna.

**3. Healthcare:**
- Securely store patient records
- Manage medical data
- Ensure only authorized access

**Roman Urdu:** Healthcare mein patient records secure store karna, medical data manage karna, authorized access ensure karna.

**4. Voting Systems:**
- Secure and transparent voting
- Accurate recording and counting
- Reduce election fraud

**Roman Urdu:** Voting systems mein secure aur transparent voting — accurate recording, fraud kam.

#### 6.4.3 Cryptocurrencies and Smart Contracts

##### 6.4.3.1 Role of Cryptocurrencies

**Definition:** Digital currencies that work without traditional banks, allowing direct transactions between people worldwide.

**Features:**
- Secure, decentralized exchange of money
- Blockchain records transactions transparently
- No middlemen needed

**Roman Urdu:** Cryptocurrencies digital currencies hain jo bina bank ke kaam karti hain — direct transactions. Blockchain se secure aur transparent. Koi middleman nahi.

##### 6.4.3.2 Smart Contracts

**Definition:** Digital agreements written in code that automatically execute when conditions are met.

**Easy Explanation:** Smart contracts are like vending machines. Put in money (condition), get a snack (result) — automatically, no human needed.

**Roman Urdu:** Smart contracts code mein likhe digital agreements hain jo conditions poori hone par automatically execute hote hain. Jaise vending machine — paisa daalo, snack mil jata hai, bina insaan ke.

**Platforms:** Ethereum enables developers to create decentralized applications (DApps) using smart contracts.

**Roman Urdu:** Ethereum par smart contracts se decentralized applications (DApps) banti hain.

**Challenges:** Need error-free code, legal systems to resolve disputes.

**Roman Urdu:** Challenges — code error-free hona chahiye, disputes ke liye legal systems chahiye.

---

### 6.5 Applications and Implications of Blockchain

#### 6.5.1 Tracking the Origin of Products

**Definition:** Blockchain tracks the origin and journey of products through the supply chain — from raw material to final customer.

**Example:** Tracking chocolate — every step (cocoa farm, factory, transport, store) recorded on blockchain, making it traceable and immutable.

**Roman Urdu:** Blockchain se product ka origin aur journey track hoti hai — raw material se customer tak. Jaise chocolate — cocoa farm, factory, transport, store sab record.

**Figure 6.3: Tracking the Origin of Chocolate Using Blockchain** — Shows supply chain steps recorded on blockchain.

**Roman Urdu:** Figure 6.3 mein chocolate ki supply chain blockchain par record dikhayi hai.

#### 6.5.2 Blockchain in Financial Services

**Definition:** Banks and financial services use blockchain to make transactions faster and safer.

**Example:** Sending money abroad — traditionally slow and expensive. Blockchain makes it quicker and cheaper.

**Roman Urdu:** Banks blockchain se transactions tez aur safe karti hain. Jaise foreign money transfer — pehle slow aur mehnga, ab tez aur sasta.

**Figure 6.4: Blockchain in Banking for Faster and Safer Transactions** — Shows identity verification, encrypted attestations, blockchain lookup.

**Roman Urdu:** Figure 6.4 mein banking mein blockchain use dikhaya — identity verify, encrypted data, blockchain lookup.

#### 6.5.3 Data Security in Blockchain

**Definition:** Blockchain ensures data is protected from unauthorized access, tampering, or loss.

**Easy Explanation:** Blockchain security is like sending a secure letter:
- **Sealing (Encryption):** Only recipient can open
- **Signing (Digital Signature):** Proves sender identity
- **Trusted Delivery (Blockchain Network):** Every step recorded
- **Multiple Copies (Decentralization):** Data stored across many computers

**Roman Urdu:** Blockchain security secure letter bhejne jaisi hai: seal (encryption), sign (digital signature), trusted delivery (blockchain network), multiple copies (decentralization).

**Figure 6.5: Cryptography Keeps Data Secure in Blockchain** — Shows plain text → encryption with key → cipher text → decryption with key → plain text.

**Roman Urdu:** Figure 6.5 mein encryption aur decryption dikhaya — plain text, cipher text, secret key.

**Class Activity (Textbook):** Imagine you have a secret code. Write a message to a friend using your code and see if they can decode it.

**Roman Urdu:** Secret code imagine karein, friend ko message likhein, dekhein decode kar sakte hain ya nahi.

**Textbook "Do You Know?":** "Big companies like Amazon and Microsoft use their powerful computers to help run blockchain networks."

**Roman Urdu:** Amazon aur Microsoft apne powerful computers se blockchain networks run karte hain.

---

### 6.6 Future Trends and Innovations

#### 6.6.1 Evolving Technologies in Cloud Computing

##### 6.6.1.1 Edge Computing

**Definition:** Brings processing power closer to data sources, reducing latency and improving efficiency. Processes data at the "edge" of the network, near the data source.

**Easy Explanation:** Instead of sending all data to a central cloud, edge computing processes it locally — like a local shop instead of a distant warehouse.

**Roman Urdu:** Edge computing data ko source ke qareeb process karta hai — central cloud tak bhejne ki bajaye. Jaise door ke warehouse ki bajaye local shop.

**Example:** Autonomous vehicles — sensors and cameras process data locally for quick responses.

**Roman Urdu:** Self-driving cars mein sensors local data process karte hain — tez response ke liye.

**Benefits:** Low latency, real-time processing, bandwidth savings.

**Roman Urdu:** Kam latency, real-time processing, bandwidth bachat.

**Textbook Tidbit:** "Edge computing is especially beneficial for applications requiring real-time processing and low latency, such as smart cities, healthcare monitoring, and industrial automation."

##### 6.6.1.2 Serverless Architectures

**Definition:** Allows developers to build and deploy applications without managing servers. Cloud provider automatically allocates resources; developers pay only for actual usage.

**Easy Explanation:** Serverless is like using a taxi — you don't own or maintain the car, just pay for the ride. The taxi company (cloud provider) handles everything.

**Roman Urdu:** Serverless mein developers servers manage nahi karte — cloud provider sab karta hai. Jaise taxi — gaari maintain nahi karni, bas kiraya dena hai.

**Example:** AWS Lambda — run code without provisioning or managing servers.

**Roman Urdu:** AWS Lambda mein code run karein bina servers manage kiye.

**Benefits:** Scalability, reduced operational complexity, cost-effective (pay per use).

**Roman Urdu:** Scalability, kam complexity, cost-effective — jitna use utna pay.

**Comparison: Edge Computing vs. Serverless**

| Feature | Edge Computing | Serverless |
|---------|----------------|------------|
| Location | Near data source | Cloud provider |
| Latency | Very low | Low to moderate |
| Use Case | Real-time (self-driving) | Event-driven apps |
| Roman Urdu | Data ke qareeb | Cloud mein, bina server |

---

## C. Important Terminology

| English Term | Easy English Meaning | Roman Urdu Meaning |
|--------------|---------------------|-------------------|
| Cloud Computing | Internet-based computing resources | Internet par computing |
| Virtualization | One machine runs multiple virtual machines | Ek machine, kai virtual |
| Scalability | Add resources when needed | Zaroorat par resources barhana |
| Elasticity | Automatic scaling | Automatic barhna/ghatna |
| On-Demand Access | Use whenever needed | Jab zaroorat ho use |
| IaaS | Infrastructure as a Service | Infrastructure service |
| PaaS | Platform as a Service | Platform service |
| SaaS | Software as a Service | Software service |
| Public Cloud | Shared cloud | Shared cloud |
| Private Cloud | Exclusive cloud | Sirf ek organization |
| Hybrid Cloud | Public + private | Dono ka mix |
| Multi-cloud | Multiple providers | Multiple providers |
| Blockchain | Decentralized ledger | Decentralized record |
| Node | Computer in network | Network ka computer |
| Ledger | Record of transactions | Transactions ka record |
| Block | Collection of transactions | Transactions ka collection |
| Transaction | Individual entry | Individual entry |
| Decentralization | No central authority | Koi central authority nahi |
| Immutability | Cannot be changed | Change nahi ho sakta |
| Consensus | Agreement among nodes | Nodes ka agreement |
| P2P Network | Direct computer-to-computer | Direct computer-to-computer |
| Cryptocurrency | Digital currency | Digital currency |
| Smart Contract | Self-executing agreement | Khud chalne wala agreement |
| Edge Computing | Processing near data source | Data ke qareeb processing |
| Serverless | No server management | Server management nahi |

## D. Differences and Comparisons

### IaaS vs. PaaS vs. SaaS

| Feature | IaaS | PaaS | SaaS |
|---------|------|------|------|
| What you get | Infrastructure | Platform | Software |
| You manage | OS, apps, data | Apps, data | Nothing |
| Provider manages | Physical infrastructure | Infrastructure, platform | Everything |
| Example | AWS, Azure | Google App Engine | Gmail, Office 365 |
| Analogy | Empty apartment | Furnished apartment | Restaurant meal |
| Roman Urdu | Khali apartment | Furnished apartment | Restaurant ka khana |

### Public vs. Private vs. Hybrid vs. Multi-cloud

| Model | Description | Example | Roman Urdu |
|-------|-------------|---------|------------|
| Public | Shared, third-party managed | AWS | Shared, third-party |
| Private | Exclusive, single organization | Bank's data center | Sirf ek organization |
| Hybrid | Public + private | Sensitive + everyday | Dono ka mix |
| Multi-cloud | Multiple providers | AWS + Azure + GCP | Multiple providers |

### Scalability vs. Elasticity

| Feature | Scalability | Elasticity |
|---------|-------------|------------|
| Definition | Add resources when needed | Automatic scaling up/down |
| Control | Manual or planned | Automatic |
| Example | Add servers for sale | Auto-scale during demand |
| Roman Urdu | Zaroorat par barhana | Automatically barhna/ghatna |

### Edge Computing vs. Serverless

| Feature | Edge Computing | Serverless |
|---------|----------------|------------|
| Location | Near data source | Cloud provider |
| Latency | Very low | Low to moderate |
| Use Case | Real-time | Event-driven |
| Roman Urdu | Data ke qareeb | Cloud mein, bina server |

### Blockchain vs. Traditional Database

| Feature | Blockchain | Traditional Database |
|---------|-----------|---------------------|
| Control | Decentralized | Centralized |
| Immutability | Yes | No (can be changed) |
| Transparency | All nodes see | Restricted access |
| Trust | Consensus-based | Central authority |
| Roman Urdu | Decentralized, immutable | Centralized, changeable |

## E. Diagrams and Flowcharts

### Cloud Services (Figure 6.1)

```
┌─────────────────────────────────────┐
│           SaaS (Software)           │  ← Gmail, Office 365
├─────────────────────────────────────┤
│          PaaS (Platform)            │  ← Google App Engine
├─────────────────────────────────────┤
│       IaaS (Infrastructure)         │  ← AWS, Azure
└─────────────────────────────────────┘
```

**Explanation:** SaaS is top layer (ready software), PaaS middle (development platform), IaaS bottom (infrastructure).

**Roman Urdu:** SaaS upar (ready software), PaaS beech (development platform), IaaS neeche (infrastructure).

### Blockchain Architecture (Figure 6.2)

```
[Block 1] ← [Block 2] ← [Block 3] ← ...
   |            |            |
 Hash         Hash         Hash
 Prev: 0     Prev: Hash1   Prev: Hash2
 Txns         Txns         Txns
```

**Explanation:** Each block contains transactions, its own hash, and previous block's hash — forming a chain.

**Roman Urdu:** Har block mein transactions, apna hash, aur previous block ka hash — chain ban jati hai.

### Supply Chain Tracking (Figure 6.3)

```
Farm → Factory → Transport → Store → Customer
  ↓        ↓         ↓         ↓        ↓
[Block] [Block]   [Block]   [Block]  [Block]
         (all recorded on blockchain)
```

**Explanation:** Every step of product journey recorded on blockchain — traceable and immutable.

**Roman Urdu:** Product ki har step blockchain par record — traceable aur immutable.

### Blockchain in Banking (Figure 6.4)

```
Identity Issuer → User → Identity Verifier
       ↓            ↓            ↓
   Attestations  Register    Lookup
       ↓         Identity    Identity
   Identity Hub ← Blockchain →
```

**Explanation:** Identity verification, encrypted attestations, blockchain lookup for secure transactions.

**Roman Urdu:** Identity verify, encrypted data, blockchain lookup — secure transactions.

### Cryptography in Blockchain (Figure 6.5)

```
Plain Text → [Encryption with Key] → Cipher Text → [Decryption with Key] → Plain Text
```

**Explanation:** Data is encrypted with a key, transmitted as cipher text, and decrypted with the key.

**Roman Urdu:** Data ko key se encrypt karte hain, cipher text bhejte hain, phir key se decrypt karte hain.

1. **Cloud Services:** IaaS (infrastructure) → PaaS (platform) → SaaS (software)

4. **P2P Network:** Direct computer-to-computer, no central server

**Roman Urdu:** Cloud services IaaS→PaaS→SaaS. Deployment public, private, hybrid, multi-cloud. Blockchain principles: decentralization, immutability, consensus. P2P direct. Edge near source. Serverless no server.

## H. Textbook Exercise Solutions

### Multiple Choice Questions (MCQs)

**1. The main benefit of edge computing:**
a) Lower cost
b) Reduced latency
c) Increased complexity
d) Enhanced security

**Answer: b) Reduced latency**
**Explanation:** Edge computing processes data near the source, reducing latency.
**Roman Urdu:** Edge computing data ke qareeb process karta hai — latency kam.

**2. A cloud deployment model with resources shared among multiple organizations with common concerns:**
a) Public Cloud
b) Private Cloud
c) Community Cloud
d) Hybrid Cloud

**Answer: c) Community Cloud**
**Explanation:** Community cloud is shared among organizations with common concerns.
**Roman Urdu:** Community cloud common concerns wale organizations ke beech shared hota hai.

**3. The advantage of using a distributed ledger in blockchain technology:**
a) Centralized control for quick decision-making
b) Easy alteration of transaction histories
c) Enhanced transparency and security through decentralized verification
d) Lower computational requirements

**Answer: c) Enhanced transparency and security through decentralized verification**
**Explanation:** Distributed ledger provides transparency and security via decentralization.
**Roman Urdu:** Distributed ledger transparency aur security deta hai — decentralized verification se.

**4. A cloud deployment model combining public and private cloud features:**
a) Public Cloud
b) Hybrid Cloud
c) Community Cloud
d) Multi-Cloud

**Answer: b) Hybrid Cloud**
**Explanation:** Hybrid cloud combines public and private.
**Roman Urdu:** Hybrid cloud public aur private ka mix hai.

**5. The purpose of a distributed ledger in blockchain:**
a) Central authority management
b) Secure and transparent data sharing among multiple participants
c) Fewer participants required
d) Data visibility only to central authority

**Answer: b) Secure and transparent data sharing among multiple participants**
**Explanation:** Distributed ledger enables secure, transparent sharing.
**Roman Urdu:** Distributed ledger multiple participants ke beech secure, transparent sharing karta hai.

**6. A cloud service offering a platform for developing, running, and managing applications without managing infrastructure:**
a) Infrastructure as a Service (IaaS)
b) Platform as a Service (PaaS)
c) Software as a Service (SaaS)
d) Data as a Service (DaaS)

**Answer: b) Platform as a Service (PaaS)**
**Explanation:** PaaS provides development platform without infrastructure management.
**Roman Urdu:** PaaS development platform deta hai bina infrastructure manage kiye.

**7. The service model enabling application deployment without server management:**
a) Infrastructure as a Service (IaaS)
b) Platform as a Service (PaaS)
c) Software as a Service (SaaS)
d) Serverless Architecture

**Answer: d) Serverless Architecture**
**Explanation:** Serverless allows deployment without server management.
**Roman Urdu:** Serverless mein bina server manage kiye deploy karte hain.

**8. The feature introduced in Blockchain 2.0 beyond cryptocurrency:**
a) Enhanced mining techniques
b) Decentralized applications and smart contracts
c) Better graphics
d) Faster internet speeds

**Answer: b) Decentralized applications and smart contracts**
**Explanation:** Blockchain 2.0 introduced smart contracts and DApps.
**Roman Urdu:** Blockchain 2.0 mein smart contracts aur DApps aaye.

**9. The primary advantage of serverless architectures:**
a) Cost savings
b) Constant server management
c) Increased hardware needs
d) Manual scaling

**Answer: a) Cost savings**
**Explanation:** Serverless saves costs by paying only for actual usage.
**Roman Urdu:** Serverless mein sirf use ke hisaab se pay — cost savings.

---

### Short Questions

**1. Analyze the role of Peer-to-Peer Networks in Blockchain. How do they function and why are they essential?**

**Answer:** P2P networks allow computers (nodes) to communicate directly without a central server. In blockchain, each node maintains a copy of the ledger, validates transactions, and shares new blocks. This decentralization ensures no single point of failure, enhances security, and enables trust without intermediaries.

**Roman Urdu:** P2P network mein computers direct baat karte hain — koi central server nahi. Blockchain mein har node ledger copy rakhta hai, transactions validate karta hai, naye blocks share karta hai. Decentralization se security aur trust milta hai.

**2. Describe the concept of immutability in blockchain. Why is it a critical feature?**

**Answer:** Immutability means once data is written to a block, it cannot be altered or deleted. This is critical because it ensures transaction history is permanent and tamper-proof, providing trust and reliability. Any attempt to change data would be detected by the network.

**Roman Urdu:** Immutability matlab ek baar data likhne ke baad change ya delete nahi ho sakta. Isse history permanent aur tamper-proof rehti hai — trust aur reliability milti hai.

**3. What is edge computing and how does it benefit data processing?**

**Answer:** Edge computing processes data near the data source (at the "edge" of the network) instead of sending it to a central cloud. Benefits: reduced latency, faster real-time processing, bandwidth savings, and improved efficiency for applications like autonomous vehicles and smart cities.

**Roman Urdu:** Edge computing data ko source ke qareeb process karta hai — central cloud tak nahi bhejta. Latency kam, real-time processing tez, bandwidth bachta hai.

**4. Describe the concept of serverless architectures.**

**Answer:** Serverless architectures allow developers to build and deploy applications without managing servers. The cloud provider automatically allocates resources, and developers pay only for actual usage. This reduces operational complexity and improves scalability.

**Roman Urdu:** Serverless mein developers servers manage nahi karte — cloud provider sab karta hai. Sirf use ke hisaab se pay karte hain. Complexity kam, scalability behtar.

**5. What advantages do serverless architectures offer to developers?**

**Answer:** Advantages: no server management, automatic scaling, cost efficiency (pay per use), reduced operational complexity, and faster deployment. Developers focus on code, not infrastructure.

**Roman Urdu:** Serverless ke faide: server management nahi, automatic scaling, cost efficiency, kam complexity, tez deployment. Developers code par focus karte hain.

**6. How does edge computing improve the efficiency of autonomous vehicles?**

**Answer:** Autonomous vehicles generate massive data from sensors and cameras. Edge computing processes this data locally in the vehicle, enabling quick responses to changing road conditions, reducing latency, and enhancing safety without relying on distant cloud servers.

**Roman Urdu:** Self-driving cars ke sensors bohat data banate hain. Edge computing is data ko vehicle mein hi process karta hai — tez response, kam latency, safety behtar.

**7. Differentiate between Elasticity and On-Demand access in cloud computing.**

**Answer:** Elasticity is the automatic scaling of resources up or down based on demand (e.g., auto-adding servers during a sale). On-demand access is the ability to use resources whenever needed without long setup (e.g., instantly renting storage). Elasticity is automatic; on-demand is user-initiated.

**Roman Urdu:** Elasticity mein resources automatically barhte/ghatte hain demand ke hisaab se. On-demand mein jab zaroorat ho foran resources mil jate hain. Elasticity automatic, on-demand user ki taraf se.

---

### Long Questions

**1. Define cloud deployment models and assess the differences among them.**

**Answer:** Cloud deployment models: (1) Public — shared, third-party managed, cost-effective but less secure (e.g., AWS). (2) Private — exclusive to one organization, secure but expensive (e.g., bank's data center). (3) Hybrid — combines public and private, flexible (e.g., sensitive data private, everyday public). (4) Multi-cloud — uses multiple providers, resilient but complex (e.g., AWS + Azure + GCP). Each model offers different trade-offs between cost, security, control, and flexibility.

**Roman Urdu:** Public shared aur sasta, private secure aur mehnga, hybrid dono ka mix, multi-cloud multiple providers. Har model ke apne faide aur nuksan hain.

**2. Classify the various types of cloud services and compare them, highlighting key distinctions.**

**Answer:** (1) IaaS — provides infrastructure (servers, storage), user manages OS/apps (e.g., AWS). (2) PaaS — provides development platform, user manages apps (e.g., Google App Engine). (3) SaaS — provides ready software, user just uses (e.g., Gmail). Key distinction: level of management — IaaS most user control, SaaS least. Analogy: IaaS = empty apartment, PaaS = furnished apartment, SaaS = restaurant meal.

**Roman Urdu:** IaaS infrastructure, PaaS platform, SaaS software. IaaS mein user zyada manage karta hai, SaaS mein kam. Jaise khali apartment, furnished apartment, restaurant ka khana.

**3. Discuss the advancements and benefits of edge computing in modern technology.**

**Answer:** Edge computing brings processing closer to data sources, reducing latency and enabling real-time decisions. Benefits: low latency (critical for autonomous vehicles), bandwidth savings (less data sent to cloud), improved reliability (local processing), and enhanced privacy (data processed locally). Advancements: smart cities, healthcare monitoring, industrial automation, and IoT devices all benefit from edge computing.

**Roman Urdu:** Edge computing data ke qareeb processing karta hai — latency kam, real-time decisions. Smart cities, healthcare, industrial automation, IoT mein faida.

**4. Explain the concept of serverless architectures and their impact on application development.**

**Answer:** Serverless architectures allow developers to deploy code without managing servers. The cloud provider automatically allocates resources and scales as needed. Impact: developers focus on code, not infrastructure; costs reduced (pay per use); scalability automatic; deployment faster. Example: AWS Lambda. Challenges: vendor lock-in, cold start delays, limited control over environment.

**Roman Urdu:** Serverless mein bina server manage kiye code deploy karte hain. Provider resources allocate karta hai. Developers code par focus, cost kam, scalability automatic. Challenges: vendor lock-in, cold start.

**5. Describe "Cloud Deployment Models" with examples.**

**Answer:** (1) Public Cloud — shared among organizations, third-party managed (e.g., AWS). (2) Private Cloud — exclusive to one organization (e.g., bank's data center). (3) Hybrid Cloud — combines public and private (e.g., sensitive data private, everyday public). (4) Multi-cloud — uses multiple providers (e.g., AWS + Azure + GCP). Each model offers different trade-offs.

**Roman Urdu:** Public shared (AWS), private exclusive (bank), hybrid mix, multi-cloud multiple providers. Har model ke apne faide.

### Important Definitions

1. **Cloud Computing:** Internet-based access to computing resources.
2. **Virtualization:** One physical machine running multiple virtual machines.
3. **Scalability:** Ability to add resources when needed.
4. **Elasticity:** Automatic scaling up/down based on demand.
5. **On-Demand Access:** Use resources whenever needed.
6. **IaaS:** Infrastructure as a Service — servers, storage, networking.
7. **PaaS:** Platform as a Service — development environment.
8. **SaaS:** Software as a Service — ready-to-use applications.
9. **Public Cloud:** Shared cloud service over internet.
10. **Private Cloud:** Exclusive cloud for one organization.
11. **Hybrid Cloud:** Combination of public and private.
12. **Multi-cloud:** Using multiple cloud providers.
13. **Blockchain:** Decentralized, distributed ledger.
14. **Decentralization:** No central authority.
15. **Immutability:** Data cannot be changed after recording.
16. **Consensus Mechanism:** Agreement among nodes.
17. **Node:** Computer in blockchain network.
18. **Ledger:** Record of all transactions.
19. **Block:** Collection of transactions.
20. **Transaction:** Individual entry in blockchain.
21. **P2P Network:** Direct computer-to-computer network.
22. **Cryptocurrency:** Digital currency using blockchain.
23. **Smart Contract:** Self-executing digital agreement.
24. **Edge Computing:** Processing near data source.
25. **Serverless:** No server management, pay per use.

# CHAPTER 7: LEGAL AND ETHICAL ASPECTS OF COMPUTING SYSTEM

## A. Chapter Introduction

**Chapter Overview:** This chapter explores the legal and ethical issues surrounding computers and the internet. You will learn about Terms of Use, privacy and security threats, the digital divide, responsible digital behavior, and ethical use of information. These topics are essential for becoming a responsible digital citizen.

**Learning Objectives (from textbook SLOs):**
- Understand fundamental legal and ethical considerations in computing systems
- Identify various privacy and security threats in digital environments
- Explain strategies for preventing and mitigating security threats
- Discuss the digital divide and its implications for different social groups
- Recognize and address bias in computing systems
- Utilize information safely and responsibly
- Assess the impact of computing on individuals and society
- Apply principles of digital citizenship and ethical conduct

**Why This Chapter Is Important:** Every day, we share personal information online, use apps, and interact on social media. Understanding the legal and ethical rules — and the threats — helps us stay safe, protect our privacy, and behave responsibly.

**Roman Urdu:** Yeh chapter batata hai ke computer aur internet use karte waqt kya legal aur ethical rules hain. Terms of Use, privacy threats, digital divide, aur responsible digital behavior seekhna zaroori hai taake hum safe rahein aur doosron ka haq na marein.

**Real-Life Applications:** Accepting Terms of Use when installing an app, recognizing phishing emails, protecting passwords, understanding why some students lack internet access, respecting copyright.

---

## B. Complete Topic-Wise Notes

### 7.1 Understanding Terms of Use

**Definition:** "Terms of Use" (also called Terms and Conditions or Terms of Service) are legal agreements that outline how a service or product can be used. They are set by the provider and must be agreed upon by the user.

**Easy Explanation:** Terms of Use are the rules for using a service. When you sign up for an app or website, you agree to follow these rules. They protect both you and the company.

**Roman Urdu:** Terms of Use wo rules hain jo batate hain ke service kaise use karni hai. Jab aap koi app ya website use karte hain, aap in rules se agree karte hain. Yeh aap aur company dono ko protect karte hain.

**Purpose:** Establish a fair and transparent relationship between provider and user, ensuring both understand their rights and obligations.

**Roman Urdu:** Maqsad hai provider aur user ke beech fair aur transparent relationship — dono apne rights aur obligations samajhein.

**Example (Textbook):** When you use a Pakistani online shopping platform like Daraz, the Terms of Use explain what you can expect and what rules you must follow.

**Roman Urdu:** Daraz use karte waqt Terms of Use batate hain ke aap kya expect kar sakte hain aur kya rules follow karne hain.

#### 7.1.1 Importance of Understanding Terms of Use

**1. Protection of Rights:**
- Terms protect users by defining their rights (privacy, service levels, redress)
- Example: Foodpanda's Terms of Use specify what happens if your order is incorrect or delayed.

**Roman Urdu:** Terms user ke rights protect karte hain — privacy, service levels, complaint. Jaise Foodpanda batata hai ke order ghalat ho to kya hoga.

**2. Clarity and Transparency:**
- Explain what the provider will deliver, under what conditions, what is not allowed
- Prevents misunderstandings
- Example: Mobile payment apps outline transaction limits, fees, and process for reporting unauthorized transactions.

**Roman Urdu:** Terms clear karte hain ke provider kya dega, kis condition mein, kya allowed nahi. Jaise payment app transaction limits aur fees batata hai.

**3. Legal Safeguards:**
- Protect businesses from misuse
- Set boundaries to avoid legal issues
- Example: Online education platform prohibits sharing course content without permission.

**Roman Urdu:** Terms business ko misuse se bachate hain. Jaise online education platform course content share karne se rokti hai.

#### 7.1.2 Common Clauses and Conditions

**1. User Obligations:**
- What is expected from the user
- Example: Careem requires valid phone number, prohibits misuse for non-transportation purposes.

**Roman Urdu:** User se kya expect kiya jata hai. Jaise Careem valid phone number mangta hai aur misuse se rokti hai.

**2. Limitations of Liability:**
- Limits provider's liability if something goes wrong
- Example: Online banking app not liable for temporary disruptions.

**Roman Urdu:** Agar kuch ghalat ho to provider ki zimmedari limit hoti hai. Jaise bank app temporary down hone par liable nahi.

**3. Privacy and Data Use:**
- How company collects, uses, and protects user data
- Example: WhatsApp's Terms outline how messages are stored and protected.

**Roman Urdu:** Company data kaise collect, use, aur protect karti hai. Jaise WhatsApp batata hai messages kaise store hote hain.

**4. Intellectual Property Rights:**
- Protect content owned by provider (logos, software, materials)
- Example: News website prohibits copying and distributing articles without permission.

**Roman Urdu:** Provider ka content (logos, software) protect hota hai. Jaise news website articles copy karne se rokti hai.

**5. Termination of Service:**
- Conditions under which provider can terminate access
- Example: Facebook can suspend accounts that violate community standards.

**Roman Urdu:** Kis condition mein provider access band kar sakta hai. Jaise Facebook hate speech par account suspend kar sakta hai.

#### 7.1.3 Legal Ramifications

**Definition:** Violating Terms of Use can lead to serious legal consequences — fines, lawsuits, or bans.

**Example:** Using software without a proper license = software piracy — legal action possible.

**Roman Urdu:** Terms violate karne se legal consequences ho sakte hain — fine, lawsuit, ya ban. Bina license software use karna piracy hai — legal action.

#### 7.1.4 Ethical Considerations

**Definition:** Ethical considerations include fairness, transparency, and respect for user rights. Terms should be clear and not deceptive.

**Example (Textbook):** When signing up for an online course, terms should clearly specify certificate eligibility, data handling, and any additional fees.

**Roman Urdu:** Ethical considerations mein fairness, transparency, aur user rights ka respect shamil hai. Terms clear hone chahiye, deceptive nahi. Jaise online course bataye certificate milega ya nahi, data kaise handle hoga, fees kya hain.

#### 7.1.5 Personal Rights

**Definition:** Personal rights include the right to privacy, right to be informed, and right to withdraw consent.

**Example (Textbook):** Under Pakistan's Personal Data Protection Bill, users can access their personal data and request corrections or deletions.

**Roman Urdu:** Personal rights mein privacy, informed hone ka haq, aur consent wapas lene ka haq shamil hai. Pakistan ke Personal Data Protection Bill ke under user apna data access kar sakta hai aur correction/delete karwa sakta hai.

**Textbook "Do You Know?":** "Some online platforms frequently update their Terms of Use. It's a good practice to review them periodically to stay informed about any changes."

**Roman Urdu:** Kuch platforms Terms of Use update karte rehte hain. Inhein waqt waqt par review karte rahein.

---

### 7.2 Privacy and Security Threats

**Definition:** Privacy and security threats are risks that can compromise personal information, financial data, and online security.

**Roman Urdu:** Privacy aur security threats wo risks hain jo personal information, financial data, aur online security ko nuksan pahuncha sakte hain.

#### 7.2.1 Types of Security Threats

##### 7.2.1.1 Spam

**Definition:** Unwanted messages received on email or phone.

**Example:** Text messages from unknown numbers offering products or services you didn't request.

**Roman Urdu:** Spam unwanted messages hain — email ya phone par. Jaise unknown numbers se promotional messages.

##### 7.2.1.2 Spyware

**Definition:** Harmful software that secretly monitors your activities on computer or mobile phone.

**Example:** Downloading a free app from an unreliable source may install spyware that monitors websites you visit and information you enter.

**Roman Urdu:** Spyware harmful software hai jo chupke se aapki activities monitor karta hai. Jaise free app download karne par spyware install ho jata hai.

##### 7.2.1.3 Cookies

**Definition:** Small files that websites place on your device. They help the website remember login details and preferences.

**Example:** Online shopping site saves your login information so you don't need to enter it every time. But cookies can also track your online activities to show targeted ads.

**Roman Urdu:** Cookies chhoti files hain jo websites aapke device par rakhti hain — login details yaad rakhne ke liye. Lekin yeh aapki activities track karke targeted ads bhi dikha sakti hain.

##### 7.2.1.4 Phishing

**Definition:** A scam where someone pretends to be a trustworthy organization to trick you into giving personal information.

**Example:** Email that looks like it's from your bank, asking you to click a link and enter account details. If you follow instructions, your money could be at risk.

**Roman Urdu:** Phishing mein koi trustworthy organization ban kar aapki personal information churata hai. Jaise bank ka fake email — link click karke account details maangta hai.

##### 7.2.1.5 Pharming

**Definition:** A sophisticated technique where users are redirected to a fake website without their knowledge.

**Example:** You type your bank's URL, but pharming sends you to a counterfeit site that looks identical. Entering login information there gives it to the scammer.

**Roman Urdu:** Pharming mein aapko bina bataye fake website par redirect kiya jata hai. Jaise bank ka URL type karo lekin fake site khul jaye — login details chori ho jayein.

**Textbook "Do You Know?":** "Over 90% of phishing attacks are carried out through email. These scams often look like legitimate communications from trusted organizations, making it crucial to verify the source before clicking on any links or providing personal information."

**Roman Urdu:** 90% phishing attacks email se hote hain. Yeh trusted organizations jaisi lagti hain — link click karne ya information dene se pehle source verify karein.

**Comparison: Phishing vs. Pharming**

| Feature | Phishing | Pharming |
|---------|----------|----------|
| Method | Fake email/message | Fake website redirect |
| User action | Click link | Type URL |
| Awareness | User may suspect | User unaware |
| Example | Fake bank email | Fake bank website |
| Roman Urdu | Fake email | Fake website |

---

#### 7.2.2 Security Threat Prevention Techniques

##### 7.2.2.1 Spam Filters

**Definition:** Tools that help keep unwanted emails out of your inbox.

**Example:** Spam filter automatically moves promotional emails to a separate folder or deletes them.

**Roman Urdu:** Spam filters unwanted emails ko inbox se door rakhte hain — promotional emails ko separate folder mein daal dete hain ya delete kar dete hain.

##### 7.2.2.2 Antivirus Software

**Definition:** Protects computer from harmful programs like spyware.

**Example:** Downloading a file — antivirus checks for hidden threats. If safe, you can open; if harmful, it alerts and prevents damage.

**Roman Urdu:** Antivirus harmful programs (spyware) se protect karta hai. File download karne par check karta hai — safe ho to khol sakte hain, harmful ho to alert karta hai.

##### 7.2.2.3 Cookie Management

**Definition:** Deciding which cookies to keep and which to delete.

**Example:** Keep cookies from favorite shopping site (so you don't log in every time), but delete cookies from unfamiliar sites.

**Roman Urdu:** Cookie management matlab decide karna kaunsi cookies rakhni hain aur kaunsi delete karni hain. Favorite site ki cookies rakhein, unfamiliar sites ki delete karein.

##### 7.2.2.4 Recognizing Phishing

**Definition:** Identifying fake emails and avoiding clicking links or providing information.

**Example:** Email appears from bank asking for account details — recognize it as fake, don't click links.

**Roman Urdu:** Phishing pehchanne ke liye fake emails identify karein — bank ka email lagta hai lekin fake hai, link click na karein, information na dein.

##### 7.2.2.5 Guarding Against Pharming

**Definition:** Checking that website address is correct and secure (look for "https://" and padlock symbol).

**Example:** Type bank's URL — check the address bar for "https://" and padlock. If something looks wrong, don't enter information.

**Roman Urdu:** Pharming se bachne ke liye website ka address check karein — "https://" aur padlock symbol dekhein. Agar kuch ghalat lage to information na dein.

**Textbook Tidbit:** "Effective online security involves using multiple strategies like spam filters, antivirus software, and careful cookie management to cover various threat types."

**Roman Urdu:** Online security ke liye multiple strategies use karein — spam filters, antivirus, cookie management.

**Comparison: Security Threats and Prevention**

| Threat | Prevention | Roman Urdu |
|--------|-----------|------------|
| Spam | Spam filters | Spam filters |
| Spyware | Antivirus software | Antivirus |
| Cookies | Cookie management | Cookie management |
| Phishing | Recognize phishing | Phishing pehchanein |
| Pharming | Check URL/padlock | URL/padlock check |

---

### 7.3 The Digital Divide and Its Impacts

**Definition:** The digital divide is the gap between individuals, communities, and regions that have access to modern Information and Communication Technology (ICT) and those that do not.

**Easy Explanation:** Some people have fast internet and devices; others don't. This gap is the digital divide. It creates unequal opportunities in education, jobs, healthcare, and social participation.

**Roman Urdu:** Digital divide wo gap hai jo un logon ke beech hai jinke paas internet aur devices hain aur jinke paas nahi. Isse education, jobs, healthcare, aur social participation mein inequality hoti hai.

#### 7.3.1 Causes of the Digital Divide

**1. Economic Barriers:**
- Many cannot afford computers, smartphones, or broadband internet
- Particularly evident in developing countries, rural areas, low-income populations

**Roman Urdu:** Economic barrier — bohat log computer, smartphone, ya internet afford nahi kar sakte. Developing countries, rural areas, low-income logon mein zyada.

**2. Geographical Barriers:**
- Access varies between urban and rural areas
- Remote/rural areas may lack internet infrastructure

**Roman Urdu:** Geographical barrier — urban aur rural areas mein farq. Remote areas mein internet infrastructure nahi hoti.

**3. Educational Barriers:**
- Lack of digital literacy
- Even when technology is available, people may not know how to use it

**Roman Urdu:** Educational barrier — digital literacy ki kami. Technology available ho lekin use karna na aata ho.

**4. Social Barriers:**
- Age, gender, disability can affect access
- Older adults may be less able; women in some regions face cultural barriers

**Roman Urdu:** Social barrier — age, gender, disability se access par asar. Buzurg kam use karte hain, kuch regions mein khawateen ko cultural barriers hain.

#### 7.3.2 Impacts of the Digital Divide

##### 7.3.2.1 Educational Inequality

**Definition:** Students without internet/devices are at a disadvantage.

**Example (Textbook):** During COVID-19, when schools shifted to online learning, students from low-income families or rural areas struggled due to lack of internet access or devices. This widened the educational gap.

**Roman Urdu:** Education mein inequality — bina internet/devices wale students peeche reh jate hain. COVID-19 mein online learning mein low-income aur rural students ko masla hua.

##### 7.3.2.2 Economic Disparities

**Definition:** Many jobs require digital skills; those without access face unemployment.

**Example:** In Pakistan, access to online job portals and digital banking is limited to urban areas, widening the economic gap between urban and rural populations.

**Roman Urdu:** Economic disparities — digital skills wale jobs milte hain, bina access wale peeche. Pakistan mein online job portals aur digital banking urban areas tak mehdood hain.

##### 7.3.2.3 Social and Civic Participation

**Definition:** Access to social media, online news, and e-government services allows civic engagement. Those without access are excluded.

**Example:** In Pakistan, e-government services are more available in cities, leaving rural populations with limited means to participate.

**Roman Urdu:** Social aur civic participation — social media, news, e-government se log engage hote hain. Bina access wale exclude ho jate hain. Pakistan mein e-government services cities mein zyada.

##### 7.3.2.4 Health Disparities

**Definition:** Digital platforms provide health information, telemedicine, appointment scheduling. Those without access are disadvantaged.

**Example:** Rural communities with limited internet may struggle to find health information or participate in telemedicine.

**Roman Urdu:** Health disparities — digital platforms se health information, telemedicine milti hai. Bina internet wale rural log health information nahi le pate.

##### 7.3.2.5 Digital Literacy Gap

**Definition:** Without access to technology, people don't develop digital skills, reinforcing the divide.

**Example:** People without computers/internet miss opportunities to learn and practice digital skills.

**Roman Urdu:** Digital literacy gap — bina technology access ke digital skills nahi banti, divide barhta hai.

#### 7.3.3 Bridging the Digital Divide

**Ways to Bridge the Gap:**

**1. Government Initiatives:**
- Build internet infrastructure in underserved areas
- Example: Government of Pakistan launched initiatives for mobile broadband in remote regions.

**Roman Urdu:** Government initiatives — internet infrastructure banayein. Pakistan government ne remote areas mein mobile broadband start kiya.

**2. Educational Programs:**
- Schools and community centers teach technology use

**Roman Urdu:** Educational programs — schools aur community centers technology use sikhayein.

**3. Public-Private Partnerships:**
- Government + businesses + nonprofits make technology affordable

**Roman Urdu:** Public-private partnerships — government, businesses, nonprofits milkar technology affordable banayein.

**Textbook "Do You Know?":** "The Government of Pakistan has launched several initiatives to increase internet access in rural areas, including the introduction of mobile broadband services in remote regions."

**Roman Urdu:** Pakistan government ne rural areas mein internet access barhane ke liye mobile broadband services start ki hain.

**Class Activity (Textbook):** Think about your community. Are there people without internet or devices? What can be done to help?

**Roman Urdu:** Apne community ke baare mein sochein — kya kuch log bina internet/devices hain? Unki madad kaise kar sakte hain?

---

### 7.4 Computing's Impact on Individuals and Society

#### 7.4.1 Positive Impacts

**1. Increased Accessibility to Information:**
- Internet provides vast information on nearly any topic
- Breaks down barriers to education and knowledge
- Example: Coursera, edX allow students from remote areas to take courses from prestigious universities.

**Roman Urdu:** Information tak access barha — internet par har topic ki information hai. Education ke barriers khatam. Jaise Coursera, edX se remote students prestigious universities ke courses le sakte hain.

**2. Improved Communication:**
- Easier and faster global connections
- Social media enables personal connections and social movements
- Example: #MeToo movement gained global traction. In Pakistan, social media raised awareness about 2014 Peshawar school attack.

**Roman Urdu:** Communication behtar — duniya bhar se jaldi connect. Social media se movements chalte hain. Jaise #MeToo, Peshawar attack par awareness.

**3. Economic Growth:**
- New industries and job opportunities
- E-commerce transformed business models
- Example: Daraz allows small businesses to reach customers nationwide and internationally.

**Roman Urdu:** Economic growth — naye industries aur jobs. E-commerce ne business badla. Jaise Daraz se chhote businesses nationwide customers tak pahunchte hain.

#### 7.4.2 Negative Impacts

**1. Digital Divide:**
- Significant portion lacks access to modern computing
- Urban-rural divide evident in Pakistan
- Unequal opportunities in education and employment

**Roman Urdu:** Digital divide — bohat log modern computing tak nahi pahunch sakte. Pakistan mein urban-rural divide. Education aur employment mein unequal opportunities.

**2. Spread of Misinformation and Fake News:**
- Can lead to social unrest
- Example: During COVID-19, misinformation about virus and vaccines spread rapidly on social media, causing confusion and fear.

**Roman Urdu:** Misinformation aur fake news — social unrest. COVID-19 mein virus aur vaccines ke baare mein galat information phaili.

**3. Privacy Concerns:**
- Increased collection and storage of personal data
- Without proper regulations, data can be misused
- Example: Leakage of citizens' data from NADRA highlights importance of data protection.

**Roman Urdu:** Privacy concerns — personal data collect aur store hota hai. Bina regulations ke misuse ho sakta hai. NADRA data leak ne data protection ki ahmiyat dikhayi.

---

### 7.5 Digital Citizenship and Ethical Considerations

**Definition:** Digital citizenship involves understanding how to behave responsibly and ethically when using digital technologies and the internet.

**Easy Explanation:** Digital citizenship means being a good citizen online — respecting others, protecting yourself, and following rules.

**Roman Urdu:** Digital citizenship matlab online achi tarah behave karna — doosron ka respect, apni protection, aur rules follow karna.

**Example (Textbook):** In Pakistan, students should communicate respectfully in online forums and social media. Avoid bullying, harassment, and spreading false information. Understand digital privacy and secure online accounts.

**Roman Urdu:** Pakistan mein students ko online respectfully communicate karna chahiye. Bullying, harassment, aur false information se bachein. Digital privacy samjhein aur accounts secure rakhein.

#### 7.5.1 Responsible Digital Behavior

**1. Using Strong Passwords:**
- Create passwords difficult for others to guess
- Example: Instead of "password123", use combination of letters, numbers, symbols

**Roman Urdu:** Strong passwords — mushkil passwords banayein. "password123" ki bajaye letters, numbers, symbols ka mix.

**2. Avoiding Phishing Scams:**
- Don't click suspicious links or open emails from unknown sources asking for personal information
- Example: If email claims to be from bank asking for account details, verify authenticity before responding.

**Roman Urdu:** Phishing scams se bachein — suspicious links click na karein, unknown emails na kholein. Bank ka email lage to verify karein.

#### 7.5.2 Ethical Use of Information

##### 7.5.2.1 Responsible Data Sharing

**Definition:** Only share personal/sensitive information when necessary and with trusted entities.

**Example:** When applying for school scholarship online, provide details only on secure, verified websites.

**Roman Urdu:** Responsible data sharing — personal information sirf zaroorat par aur trusted entities ke saath share karein. Jaise scholarship application secure website par hi dein.

##### 7.5.2.2 Ethical Issues

**1. Respecting Copyright:**
- Don't copy or use someone else's work without permission
- Example: When writing research paper, don't copy text from online articles without citing the source.

**Roman Urdu:** Copyright respect karein — doosron ka kaam bina permission copy na karein. Jaise research paper mein online article se text copy na karein bina source cite kiye.

**2. Avoiding Plagiarism:**
- Properly attribute ideas and information to original authors
- Mention source of quotes/ideas in assignments

**Roman Urdu:** Plagiarism se bachein — ideas aur information original authors ko attribute karein. Assignments mein quotes/ideas ka source batayein.

#### 7.5.3 Cybersecurity Awareness

**Definition:** Understanding and implementing practices to protect oneself from online threats.

**Textbook "Do You Know?":** "A Cyber-Crime can be reported at NR3C-FIA available at https://www.nr3c.gov.pk"

**Roman Urdu:** Cyber crime NR3C-FIA par report kar sakte hain — https://www.nr3c.gov.pk

#### 7.5.4 Collaborative Problem Solving

##### 7.5.4.1 Human-Machine Collaboration

**Definition:** Working with technology to solve problems.

**1. Using Tools:**
- Leverage software and digital tools
- Example: Students use spreadsheet software to analyze data for a project.

**Roman Urdu:** Tools use karein — software aur digital tools se kaam asaan. Jaise students spreadsheet se data analyze karte hain.

**2. Combining Skills:**
- Integrate human skills with machine capabilities
- Example: Use data analysis tools to interpret complex data while applying human judgment.

**Roman Urdu:** Skills combine karein — human skills aur machine capabilities. Jaise data analysis tools se data interpret karein aur human judgment apply karein.

##### 7.5.4.2 Teamwork and Knowledge Sharing

**1. Group Projects:**
- Work together, each contributing expertise
- Example: In science class group project, students collaborate and share research findings.

**Roman Urdu:** Group projects — mil kar kaam karein, har koi apna expertise de. Jaise science project mein students research share karte hain.

**2. Sharing Insights:**
- Exchange ideas and knowledge with peers
- Participate in study groups or discussions

**Roman Urdu:** Insights share karein — ideas aur knowledge peers ke saath exchange karein. Study groups mein participate karein.

#### 7.5.5 Accessibility and User Feedback

##### 7.5.5.1 Accessibility

**Definition:** Designing digital content so everyone, including people with disabilities, can use it.

**1. Readable Fonts:**
- Use clear, large fonts
- Example: Arial or Times New Roman, avoid decorative styles

**Roman Urdu:** Readable fonts — clear aur large fonts use karein. Jaise Arial, Times New Roman.

**2. Alternative Text for Images:**
- Provide descriptions for images so visually impaired users can understand
- Example: Include descriptive text for charts/graphs in a report

**Roman Urdu:** Images ke liye alternative text — visually impaired users ke liye description. Jaise charts/graphs ke liye descriptive text.

##### 7.5.5.2 User Feedback

**1. Surveys and Forms:**
- Collect feedback to improve digital content
- Example: After creating website, ask users for feedback and make adjustments.

**Roman Urdu:** Surveys se feedback lein — digital content behtar banayein. Jaise website banane ke baad users se feedback lein.

**2. Direct Communication:**
- Engage with users to understand needs
- Example: Ask classmates for input on presentation and use feedback to improve.

**Roman Urdu:** Direct communication — users se baat karein, unki needs samjhein. Jaise classmates se presentation par feedback lein.

**Textbook Note:** "In 2020, Pakistan introduced the Personal Data Protection Bill, which aims to protect citizens' data and ensure their privacy. This bill requires organizations to obtain explicit consent before collecting personal data and mandates secure storage."

**Roman Urdu:** 2020 mein Pakistan ne Personal Data Protection Bill introduce kiya — citizens ka data protect karna aur privacy ensure karna. Organizations ko explicit consent lena hoga aur data secure store karna hoga.

| Termination | Ending service access | Service access band |
| Spam | Unwanted messages | Unwanted messages |
| Spyware | Secret monitoring software | Chupke monitoring software |
| Cookies | Small files storing preferences | Chhoti files preferences store |
| Phishing | Fake email scam | Fake email scam |
| Pharming | Fake website redirect | Fake website redirect |
| Spam Filter | Blocks unwanted emails | Unwanted emails block |
| Antivirus | Protects from malware | Malware se protect |
| Cookie Management | Controlling cookies | Cookies control |
| Digital Divide | Gap in technology access | Technology access ka gap |
| Economic Barrier | Cost prevents access | Kharcha access roke |
| Geographical Barrier | Location prevents access | Location access roke |
| Educational Barrier | Lack of digital literacy | Digital literacy ki kami |
| Social Barrier | Age/gender/disability | Age/gender/disability |
| Digital Citizenship | Responsible online behavior | Online responsible behavior |
| Copyright | Legal right to creative work | Creative work ka legal right |
| Plagiarism | Using others' work without credit | Bina credit doosron ka kaam |

| Accessibility | Usable by everyone | Sab ke liye usable |

## D. Differences and Comparisons

### Phishing vs. Pharming

| Feature | Phishing | Pharming |
|---------|----------|----------|
| Method | Fake email/message | Fake website redirect |
| User action | Click link | Type URL |
| Awareness | May suspect | Unaware |
| Example | Fake bank email | Fake bank website |
| Roman Urdu | Fake email | Fake website |

### Spam vs. Spyware

| Feature | Spam | Spyware |
|---------|------|---------|
| Type | Unwanted messages | Monitoring software |
| Purpose | Advertising/scam | Steal information |
| Prevention | Spam filter | Antivirus |
| Roman Urdu | Unwanted messages | Monitoring software |

### Positive vs. Negative Impacts of Computing

| Positive | Negative |
|----------|----------|
| Information access | Digital divide |
| Better communication | Misinformation |
| Economic growth | Privacy concerns |
| Roman Urdu: Faide | Roman Urdu: Nuksan |

### Digital Divide Causes

| Cause | Description | Roman Urdu |
|-------|-------------|------------|
| Economic | Cannot afford technology | Kharcha afford nahi |
| Geographical | Rural areas lack infrastructure | Rural areas mein infrastructure nahi |
| Educational | Lack of digital literacy | Digital literacy ki kami |
| Social | Age/gender/disability | Age/gender/disability |
